# run9 英語レビュー: ワールド15・16（レッスン 421〜480）

- 日付: 2026-11-27
- やりかた: docs/run9/BRIEF.md の「Review method」。lint → 3人の レビュアー（A=アメリカの 小学校の 先生 / B=こどもの ESL・言語習得の 専門家 / C=台本を 読む 声優）を べつべつに 書いて から 決める。2人 いじょうが 指摘 or 客観的な まちがい（objective）だけ なおす。10 レッスンずつ 6 グループで レビュー
- lint（`npm run english:lint -- --from 421 --to 480`）: **まえ error 0 / warn 102 / info 8 → あと error 0 / warn 1 / info 5**（まえの warn: Okay・okay 93・football 7・thumbs up の ゆれ 1・ながい 文 1。あとの warn 1: L466 の おはなしの 文「He had a big castle and lots of gold, but he was lonely.」〈13 語〉。but で つなぐ おはなしの 文で、こどもが 言う 文では ないので のこす）
- なおした 欄: **586**（パッチ 657 件。共通の 機械的な なおし 48・ブロックの きめごと 337〈316 欄〉・レビュアー 239・あとの そろえ 33）。なおした レッスン: **60 / 60**
- 60 レッスン ぜんぶに `"reviewed": "run9"` を つけた
- パッチは ぜんぶ「before が いまの 値と おなじ ときだけ あてる」で あてた（はずれ 0）。60 レッスン ぜんぶで スライドの 検証（unknownSlideItems）・台本の 長さ（180〜240 秒）・英語の わりあいを たしかめた（ALL OK）

## 共通の きめごと（ブロック全体）
1. 日本語の なかの 英語の あとの 2重の 記号（「What are you doing?。」「brighter.。」）→ 英語がわの 記号だけ のこす（W01-14 と おなじ）
2. 話しことばの 2択「Yes, I am! or No, I'm not.」「Yes, I did. Or, No, I didn't.」→「Yes, I am! Or no, I'm not.」、英語字幕は「A / B」（W03-14 と おなじ）。※ W13-14 の 道具では「We're... or I'm...」が「or i'm...」に なる バグが あった（「I'm...」の「...」で 名前の リストに あたらなかった）。さいごの「.」を とって から しらべる ように なおした
3. 「Okay / okay」→「OK」（スタイルガイドの 決まった 形。lint の 93 warn。teacherScript の「Okay, ～」・会話・スライド・L473 の 学校の 表現も）
4. 文の なかの「Now, Ring the bell.」「Then, Look ～」→ 小文字（say・英語字幕・teacherScript。TPR の 項目と スライドは 大文字の まま。名前は そのまま）。グループで「After that, Plant」「Finally, Show」も 小文字に した
5. 「Give a thumbs up.」→「Give a thumbs-up.」（L473 の TPR。W01-14 と そろえた）
6. イギリスふうの ほめことば: L444「Lovely work tonight.」→「Great work tonight.」・「Brilliant, little stars!」→「Great job, little stars!」、L455「Lovely work!」→「Great work!」、L478「Lovely work!」→「Nice work!」、L452・L474「Brilliant!」→「Fantastic!」、L445「That's lovely!」→「That's so nice!」（会話・aiTask の ねらいの 文・スライドも）。グループで L426「Baa-rilliant!」→「Baa! Great job!」、L422・L423「Run on the spot」→「Run in place」
7. ベルの 音「Ding, dong!」→「Ding-dong!」（L456）
8. まえの レッスンを「きのう」と きめつけない: L442・L472「Yesterday we ～」→「Last time, we ～」（L452 は「きのう」を ならう レッスンなので そのまま）
9. L450・L480 の teacherScript「speed TPR」→「speed challenge」（say は もう speed challenge。W13-14 と おなじ）
10. L431 フォニックス「football」→「classroom」（class + room）。サッカーの レッスンで football は イギリスの いいかた（アメリカでは べつの スポーツ）。おなじ 複合語の homework・playground と そろう。focus・sounds・tipJa・testPoints・まとめ・台本・スライドを あわせ、tests/phonics-w15.test.ts も なおした（W07-08 L239 と おなじ 型の なおし）
11. あとの そろえ（グループの 報告から）:
    - 過去形の よびかた「sing の むかしは sang」「むかしの かたち」「とくべつな むかしの ことば」→「すぎた かたち」に そろえた（L456〜463・L469・L471・L474・L476。むかし は「ずっと まえ」の いみで、過去形の 名まえに あわない、と 2つの グループが べつべつに 指摘。ワールド16 では もう「すぎた かたち」が 30 回 つかわれて いて、W17〜31 も おなじ）。「むかし の ことは ed!」（L451 の ずっと まえの はなし）や「むかしむかし」は そのまま。※ L451-460 の グループの メモに ある「sing は sang」の パッチは いちど はずし、この そろえで「sing の すぎた かたちは sang」に した（メモの 決定と 実際の 値が ちがうのは ここだけ）
    - 「did の あとは ed なし」→「did の あとは もとの かたち」（L454・L475。Did you see の ように ed の ない ことばにも あう）
    - y の はじめの 音の かきかた「yeh」→「yuh」（L443・L444 の teacherScript、L449。TTS が ちがう 音で よむので、L443〜445 の 台本の「yuh」に そろえた）
    - まとめの 文の なかの ものがたりの ことば「Once upon a time, First, then, and Finally」「Yesterday, Then, and Finally」→ 小文字（L480・L477。L467 と おなじ）

## 学習項目 ID が かわった もの（記録が あたらしく なる）
- L431 フォニックス「football」→「classroom」
- L473 TPR「Give a thumbs up.」→「Give a thumbs-up.」
- L473 表現「Are you okay now? ― Yes, I'm fine.」→「Are you OK now? ― Yes, I'm fine.」

## のこした こと（1人だけの 指摘・ほかの ワールドにも ある・あとで 決める）
- 「Well done!」（L467）は アメリカでも ふつう
- 学習項目の ことば: L465「treasure box」（アメリカでは treasure chest の ほうが おおい）、L469 TPR「Show me surprised.」、L470 TPR「Speak loudly.」、L449 TPR「Blow it up high.」。どれも まちがいでは なく ID が かわるので のこした（全体で 決める）
- L466 の 13 語の おはなしの 文（lint warn 1）
- フォニックスの tipJa の「重ねる」（漢字）: おなじ tipJa に「母音・子音」も ある ので、ほかの 10 か所は そのまま（L475 だけ グループが かさねる に した）
- 「Hello, my stars!」（エマ先生の あいさつ）、「What's the past of buy?」、わざと まちがえた かたち（goed・eated・buyed・flied）、文字の よみあげ（「E D」）
- L450 の「Live.」だけの よみ: 「Like a live show!」を たして、TTS が 動詞の live と よまない ように した。のこる ぶんは よみかたの ヒントで 決める
- 日本語の「かれ・かのじょ」（L436 ほか）: ほかの ワールドでも つかって いるので のこした
- ほかの ワールドで みつけた もの: 「Ms. Emma says / asks」（エマ先生 じぶんの 語りで。W17・W20・W26〜40）、world-24「Show time!」、world-40 の「むかしは」

## レビュアーの メモ（グループごと・英語）

### Run 9 review notes: L421-430 (world 15, Stage 2)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide). I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (double end marks, quiz "A / B" subtitles, "Yes, I am! Or no, I'm not." says, OK, thumbs-up, US praise, Ding-dong, "Last time", "speed challenge", lowercase after "Now, / Then, / Next, / First,") or the central decisions (Japanese in `say`, letter spell-outs like "C E", "T T, P P", "i-n-g"). There are 39 patches in 10 lessons (every lesson has at least one change). There are no learning-item ID changes and no title changes. Main themes: a UK phrase ("Run on the spot" -> "Run in place", L422/L423); wrap-up sentences over the 12-word limit, or with a dangling second clause (L421, L423, L430); unclear teacher instructions ("Swap them!", "I'll ask, and you do it. What are you doing? Pull the string!", "Answer me with the truth"); odd collocations ("Yummy work", "in the town", "call her on a video call", "bird house"); and the TTS non-word "Baa-rilliant" (a pun on UK "brilliant"). Every new English sentence has 12 words or fewer. The check script passes: ALL OK. All ten lessons are 219-228 s long with 97-98% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L421 The Wind Is Blowing!
- A: The arrival on the plains and the hat scene are natural ("I've got it! Here you go, Haru."). "Right now needs is plus ing." (seg19) is not something a teacher would say. The seg20 wrap-up is one 20-word sentence, and "and add ing" loses its subject.
- B: The quiet-e rule is introduced clearly, and the quiz contrast "The wind blowing / The wind is blowing" fits. Same flag on the seg20 length (over the Stage 2 limit). "Let the ing ring in your nose" is a fine cue for the nasal /ŋ/.
- C: In seg9, "Make, wave, smile, shine end with a quiet E" sounds like four commands until the verb arrives. Seg19 "Right now needs" trips. The seg20 sentence has no place to breathe.
- Decision: seg9 -> "Make, wave, smile, and shine end with a quiet E." (A+C). seg19 -> "For right now, use is plus ing." (A+C). seg20 split into "You can say the wind is blowing and the grass is moving. And you can add ing to make and smile." (A+C). The subtitleJa lines already match and are kept.

#### L422 What Are You Doing?
- A: The kite scene and Birimo flying off with the string are fun and natural. "Run on the spot" (seg12) is British (US: "run in place"). In seg13, "I'll ask, and you do it. What are you doing? Pull the string!" does not work: a question is followed by commands.
- B: Same flag on seg13: a child hears "What are you doing?" and then has to act, not answer, which confuses the target question. The ride/hide/slide/write set is a good second step for the quiet-e rule.
- C: Reads well ("Wheee, I'm sliding down the hill!"). "Hold it tight, or your kite flies away!" is fine aloud (single note, kept).
- Decision: seg12 "Run on the spot" -> "Run in place" (objective, US). seg13 -> "I'll say it, and you do it.", with "What are you doing?" dropped from say/subtitleEn/subtitleJa (A+B).

#### L423 Look! She's Running!
- A: The race commentary is natural ("On your mark, get set, go!", "Kiko wins! Haru is second, and the rabbit is third."). Seg11 also has "Run on the spot". Seg20 runs "and you can cheer, You can do it!" into one 16-word sentence.
- B: The she's/he's contrast and the Haru quiz are clear. "Look! He's jumping. Look! She's dancing." for a friend in a race is playful but OK (single flag, kept).
- C: The seg20 run-on "you can cheer, You can do it!" blurs where the quote starts. Everything else reads well.
- Decision: seg11 "Run on the spot" -> "Run in place" (objective, US). seg20 split: "You can say she's running and he's running. And you can cheer, You can do it!" (A+C).

#### L424 Are You Having Fun?
- A: The picnic and the sandwich joke are natural ("No, I'm not! I'm just smelling it."). "Answer me with the truth." (seg17) is not idiomatic. "Yummy work today!" (seg20) is an odd collocation, and its JA おいしい がんばり is odd too.
- B: In seg8, "You are having fun. Swap them!" does not say what to swap, and that is the core point of the lesson. Same flag on seg17. The short-answer point is clear.
- C: Seg17 "Answer me with the truth" is clunky to say, and "Yummy work" lands flat. "Bye-bye, E, hello, ing!" is fun aloud. "the wind is soft today" in the script is a little unusual (single flag, kept).
- Decision: seg8 "Swap them!" -> "Swap you and are!", JA you と are を いれかえて！ (A+B). seg17 -> "Be honest!" (A+B+C). The JA ほんとうの きもちで こたえてね still fits. seg20 -> "What a yummy picnic!", JA おいしい ピクニックだったね！ (A+C).

#### L425 Windy Day Video Call
- A: The video call is natural ("That one looks like a horse!", "I'm drinking tea in a cafe."). The script "Today Ms. Emma is in the town" should be "in town", and "Let's call her on a video call" is redundant.
- B: The I'm/we're contrast and the skate/jump comparison are well staged. The privacy tip is good. EN and JA match.
- C: The script's "call her on a video call" repeats call. Segments read well ("Ring, ring!", "Crystal clear, like a great video call!").
- Decision: teacherScript "in the town" -> "in town", "Let's call her on a video call." -> "Let's give her a video call." (A+C).

#### L426 Horses Are Eating Grass
- A: The farm scene is warm ("Aww, so cute!", "Here you are, horses!"). "Baa-rilliant!" (seg19) puns on "brilliant", which the block replaced as UK praise. "Fun fact, one sheep..." wants a colon.
- B: The doubling rule and the sheep singular/plural fact are clear and well timed. "They're trotting slowly." with JA あるいて is loose (trotting is faster than walking), but it is a learning item and fine for kids (kept).
- C: TTS will read "Baa-rilliant" badly. "T T, P P, T T, P P." is the block spell-out pattern (kept).
- Decision: seg19 -> "Fun fact: one sheep, two sheep, the word doesn't change. Baa! Great job!", JA メェー！ よく できました！ (A+C).

#### L427 The Rabbit Is Behind the Rock
- A: The hide-and-seek scene is natural ("Ready or not, here I come!", "I found you, Mr. Fox!"). The script's "let's be quiet like hunters" is the wrong image for friendly animal watching, and seg0 already calls the kids "detectives".
- B: Same flag on "hunters" (a fox is already chasing the rabbit, so a hunting image is not warm). The hand gestures for behind / in front of / next to are good. In seg7, "Oh, good." has no speaker (it is Riko's line), but it reads fine (single flag, kept).
- C: Reads well ("Peekaboo!", "Super sneaky!").
- Decision: teacherScript "quiet like hunters" -> "quiet like detectives" (A+B).

#### L428 Between the Trees
- A: The bird-watching scene is natural ("Which trees? There are so many."). "A cozy little bird house" (seg4) sounds like a birdhouse, the wooden box people build. In seg20, "You can say between, and between this and that." is confusing.
- B: Same flag on "bird house" (a wrong picture for nest). "Between needs two things, one on each side" is clear. In seg7, "It's in its nest. Look, three eggs!" has no "I say" attribution (single flag, kept).
- C: The seg20 "between, and between this and that" stumbles. "One time, carefully!" is fine.
- Decision: seg4 -> "A cozy little bird home made of sticks." (A+B). seg20 -> "You can say between the big tree and the small tree.", with subtitleJa to match (A+C).

#### L429 What Is the Dog Doing?
- A: The farm dog scene is natural ("Can I pet your dog? Always ask first, just like Riko!"). "Pawsome work!" is a common kid pun and OK.
- B: The it's/is-it point and the "Yes, it is / Yes, it does" quiz fit well. The JA in seg3 has すきま (a gap or crack) for a dug space in the ground. That is the wrong word.
- C: Reads well ("Woof woof, see you!"). The "g-g, g-g, g-g, b-b" spell-out in the script is the block pattern (kept).
- Decision: seg3 subtitleJa すきま -> くぼみ (objective, JA). No English change.

#### L430 Animal Watching Report
- A: The report day and Birimo's cookie report are fun and natural. "Any animals are OK." (seg17) is unnatural. The seg20 wrap-up is a 15-word sentence. Seg13 is titled "Let's move!" but it is the speed challenge.
- B: The three-rule ing review is clear. Same flag on seg20 length. The aiTask "What animals did you watch?" could be "see", but "watch" ties to animal watching (single flag, kept).
- C: Seg17 "Any animals are OK" is clunky aloud. "Ing, ing, ing!" may be read oddly by TTS, but it is a short chant (single flag, kept).
- Decision: seg13 slide.title "Let's move!" -> "Speed challenge" (objective, consistency). seg17 -> "Any animal is OK." (A+C). seg20 split: "You can say what animals are doing and where. And you know three ing rules." (A+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **"Run on the spot"** (UK) -> "Run in place": fixed in L422 seg12 and L423 seg11. The JA その場で and the tpr action text are fine. Check L431-480 and earlier worlds for "on the spot".
- **Wrap-up run-ons** ("You can say X, and you can Y ...", 15-20 words) in the seg20 template: fixed in L421, L423, L430. Expect the same template elsewhere in L431-480.
- **Slide title mismatch**: L430 seg13 had "Let's move!" for a speed challenge. A quick grep for speed-challenge segments with other titles in L431-480 is worth doing.
- **Puns on praise words** ("Baa-rilliant", L426): non-words for TTS, and "brilliant" was replaced block-wide. Watch for other puns (e.g. "Pawsome" in L429 was kept because it is common US kid talk).
- **Unattributed lines in the "Talk" summaries** (L427 seg7 "Oh, good.", L428 seg7 "It's in its nest.") were kept. They are not errors, but they could get "Riko says," / "I say," in a later pass.
- Kept on purpose: "Let the ing ring in your nose" (L421); "Hold it tight, or your kite flies away!" (L422); "They're trotting slowly." with JA あるいて (L426 learning item); "Pawsome work!" (L429); "Ing, ing, ing!" (L430); third-person "Ms. Emma wants to know..." in L424/L425 scripts.

### Run 9 review notes: L431-440 (world 15, Stage 2)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide). I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (double end marks, quiz "A / B" subtitles, OK, thumbs-up, US praise, Ding-dong, Last time, "speed challenge", lowercase after "Now, / Then, / Next, / First,", L431 football->classroom). There are 52 patches in 10 lessons (every lesson has at least one change). There are no learning-item ID changes and no title changes. The most common fixes are: "tell ~" used for "talk about / give / say" (L431 "Tell every day from now", L438 "tell a reason", L439 "Tell the differences", L440 "tell your idea"); the made-up term "two-word words" (L432, L433, L435 -> "two-part words"); "Congratulations, everyone!" as an opener with nothing to congratulate (L435, L440); TTS-unfriendly fill-in models with a bare "ing" (L438, L439 seg17); and the block "First, in the picture" lowercase that now reads as "inside the picture" (L436, L440 -> "First, say In the picture"). Objective fixes: L435 calls a rainbow a "plains animal", L437 has a capital "In" mid-sentence, L431 JA チクッ for a clock tick, and three speed-challenge slides titled "Let's move!". Every new English sentence has 12 words or fewer. The check script passes: ALL OK. The touched lessons are 222-228 s long with 97-99% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L431 Every Day or Now?
- A: The soccer scene and the habit / now contrast are natural ("Do you play on weekends, too?", "Pass it to me!"). In seg1, "Tell every day from now." uses "tell A from B" (= distinguish), which is adult and abstract for a goal line. "Like, I read every day." in seg17 is casual but fine.
- B: Same flag on seg1: the child cannot tell what to do. The every day / usually vs now / Look! cue words are clear, and the quiz contrast is right. In the seg4 JA, チクッ is a needle-prick sound, not a clock tick (カチッ).
- C: Reads well ("Fast feet, fast feet!", "Goal! Woo-hoo!"). "This very second, tick!" is fine aloud.
- Decision: seg1 say/subtitleEn "Tell every day from now." -> "Every day or right now? Pick the right sentence." (A+B). seg4 subtitleJa チクッ -> カチッ (objective, JA).

#### L432 Pass the Ball!
- A: The game calls are real ("I'm open! Over here!", "Good pass!", "Good game, everyone! Let's shake hands."). "Today is a mini soccer game." in the script makes the day equal a game. "two-word words" (seg9) is not something a teacher says.
- B: The kind-words theme is well placed, and the compound words are concrete. "Pass the ball means kick it to me" is simple enough. The JA いくよ for "Here you go" fits the moment.
- C: "two-word words" stumbles (word-word). "Today is a mini soccer game" has a flat, odd stress. The rest reads well ("So many balls!").
- Decision: teacherScript "Today is a mini soccer game." -> "Today we have a mini soccer game." (A+C). seg9 say/subtitleEn "two-word words" -> "two-part words" (A+C).

#### L433 Keep Going!
- A: The marathon cheers are natural ("You're almost there! I can see the finish line!"). In seg8, "Keep going in the middle of the race." reads as an order to the child, not as "say Keep going". "help a friend never give up" (seg20) is awkward. "A very, very long race" is the stacked "very" from the style guide.
- B: Same flag on seg8: the when-to-say-what idea gets lost. The JA ほこりに おもうわ (line 5) is translationese; じまん is what an adult says to a child. "Cheering words give people power" is a little translated, but clear (single flag, kept).
- C: "two-word words" again (seg9). seg8 has odd stress with the phrases at the front. "A very, very long race" is OK aloud (single flag on its own, but A+B agree from the style guide).
- Decision: seg2 say/subtitleEn -> "A really, really long race." (A+B). seg8 say/subtitleEn -> "In the middle of the race, say Keep going. Near the end, say You're almost there. After the race, say You did it!" (A+C). seg9 say/subtitleEn -> "More two-part words." (A+C). seg20 say/subtitleEn -> "..., so your friend won't give up." (A+C). conversation.lines[5].ja みんな ほこりに おもうわ -> わたしたちの じまんよ (objective, JA).

#### L434 Who's Winning?
- A: The tug-of-war is lively ("Heave, ho!", "Ha ha, everyone is on the grass!"). "Then answer short." (seg1) is not standard English. Birimo says "Now the blue team is winning! Who's winning now?", which answers his own question, but it rehearses the target (single flag, kept).
- B: Same flag on "answer short". The short-answer point ("You don't need to say winning again") is clear, and the is / does quiz contrast is good. The seg19 JA チャンピオン for "winner" is close enough (kept).
- C: Reads well. "Who is winning? The red team is." has a good rhythm.
- Decision: seg1 say/subtitleEn "Then answer short." -> "Keep the answer short." (A+B).

#### L435 Sports Day on the Plains
- A: The reporter frame is fun ("Back to you, Ms. Emma!", "Whoosh! I'm flying like a butterfly!"). The script opens with "Congratulations, everyone!" with nothing to congratulate. "to catch ears" (seg8) is a translation of みみを つかむ, not English. seg9 calls rainbow a "plains animal".
- B: Same flags on "Congratulations" and "catch ears". rainbow is not an animal (also in the JA いきもの and in tipJa), which is a factual error. The is / are contrast is clear.
- C: In seg7, Kiko's lines have no speaker cue, and "Then, what a race!" blends narration into her line. "catch ears" is also hard to make sense of aloud.
- Decision: teacherScript "Congratulations, everyone!" -> "Hello, everyone!" (A+B). seg7 say/subtitleEn -> "Kiko goes on. ... Kiko ends, What a race! ..." (A+C; the JA already marks Kiko). seg8 say/subtitleEn "to catch ears" -> "to get everyone's attention" (A+B+C). seg9 say/subtitleEn -> "Let's review two-part words from the plains.", subtitleJa そうげんの いきもので -> そうげんで みつけた, and tipJa 草原の いきものの 複合語 -> 草原で みつけた 複合語 (objective).

#### L436 In the Picture
- A: The studio talk is natural ("Look, that cloud is me!", "It looks just like you!"). "Please look at this picture." (line 0) is the stiff please-command from a teacher. In the script, Ms. Emma says "Ms. Emma is pretending to be the painter." about herself. In seg8, "First, in the picture." (lowercase from the block fix) reads as "inside the picture".
- B: Same flag on "Please look". The apostrophe explanation is clear. The JA かれ / かのじょ in expressions and lines is textbook style, but the product uses it in many worlds, so it is kept (reported below). The quiz distractor "He'm" is intentional.
- C: In seg9, the JA part starts with a bare "'", which TTS cannot read, so the spoken sentence has no subject. The third-person self-reference in the script also sounds odd. Same flag on seg8.
- Decision: conversation.lines[0].en -> "Take a look at this picture." (A+B). teacherScript "Ms. Emma is pretending" -> "I'm pretending" (A+C). seg8 say/subtitleEn -> "First, say In the picture. Next, say who is doing what." (A+C). seg9 say ' は -> アポストロフィは (B+C; the subtitleJa keeps the mark for display).

#### L437 On the Left, On the Right
- A: The festival picture and the Birimo joke work ("Don't worry, Birimo. You're next!"). In the script, "And In the middle" has a capital mid-sentence. "If your friend is lost, help them." means lost in a place, which is not the picture context.
- B: Same flag on "is lost". The L-with-your-left-hand trick is correct and helpful. "On the right, they're eating lunch." has no antecedent for they, but it is the learning item and it is clear with the picture (kept).
- C: Reads well ("Left and right, all right!", "Boing!").
- Decision: teacherScript "And In the middle" -> "And in the middle" (objective), and "If your friend is lost" -> "If your friend can't find something" (A+B).

#### L438 What's Happening?
- A: The newspaper photo chat is natural ("Extra, extra!", "Let's help them tomorrow!" / "That's a good idea, Birimo!"). "You can tell a reason, too." in the script is unidiomatic ("give a reason"). "Great reporting today!" (seg20) is a loose fit for reading a newspaper (single flag, kept).
- B: Same flag on "tell a reason". The seg17 frame "Some people are... ing." has no real model sentence. The seg13 speed-challenge slide is titled "Let's move!", which does not match the other lessons.
- C: TTS will read a bare "ing" in seg17. "The i in is is missing" in the script stumbles on "is is"; seg9 already says "goes away".
- Decision: teacherScript "tell a reason" -> "give a reason" (A+B), "The i in is is missing" -> "The i in is goes away" (B+C). seg17 say/subtitleEn/subtitleJa -> "Then answer with ing, like, Some people are running." (B+C). seg13 slide.title -> "Speed challenge" (objective).

#### L439 Spot the Difference
- A: The game is natural ("Found one!", "Found two! Three more differences. Keep going!"). "Tell the differences." (seg1) and "You can tell differences" (seg20) use tell for talk about, the same template the L381-390 review fixed.
- B: Same flag on "tell". The isn't / aren't contrast and the quiz are correct ("They isn't" is an intentional distractor). The seg17 model "the... is... ing" gives no full sentence. The seg13 slide title is "Let's move!".
- C: TTS will read "the... is... ing" and "isn't... ing" as broken pieces. "n-apostrophe-t" in the script reads OK as letters (single flag, kept).
- Decision: seg1 and seg20 say/subtitleEn "tell (the) differences" -> "talk about (the) differences" (A+B). seg17 say/subtitleEn/subtitleJa -> "Like, In my picture, the dog is running. In your picture, it isn't running." (B+C). seg13 slide.title -> "Speed challenge" (objective).

#### L440 Picture Talk Show
- A: The show talks are warm ("It isn't very exciting." / "But it's very peaceful, Birimo."). The script opens with "Congratulations, everyone!" again. "First, in the picture, and what's happening." reads as "inside the picture". "You're show stars!" (seg13) is not idiomatic. "tell your idea" (seg18) is unidiomatic. "Haru, please start." is acceptable (single flag, kept).
- B: Same flags on "Congratulations" and "tell your idea". The three-part frame is clear. The seg13 slide title is "Let's move!".
- C: The seg8 spoken JA "I think は と おもう" sounds like a stutter (the 〜 is only in the subtitle). "show stars" is flat aloud.
- Decision: teacherScript "Congratulations, everyone!" -> "Hello, everyone!" (A+B), "First, in the picture, and" -> "First, say In the picture and" (A+C). seg8 say -> "I think は おもう という いみ." (B+C). seg13 say/subtitleEn -> "You're the stars of the show!" (A+C) and slide.title -> "Speed challenge" (objective). seg18 say/subtitleEn -> "You want to say what you think about the picture." (A+B).

#### Block-wide notes
- **"two-word words"**: there are 6 uses in world 15. I changed the 3 in L432, L433 and L435 to "two-part words". The other 3 are outside this range (probably L441-450), so please make them the same.
- **Speed-challenge slide titles**: seg13 slide.title is still "Let's move!" in L421-430 (one lesson) and L441-450 (one lesson), in the dumps at L421-430.txt:1941 and L441-450.txt:1775. I fixed L438-440 to "Speed challenge".
- **Lowercase after "First,"**: this block fix creates ambiguous lines when the next word starts a quoted phrase ("First, in the picture." = inside the picture). I fixed these with "First, say In the picture" in L436 seg8 and L440 teacherScript. Check other lessons for "First, in ~ / Next, who ~" patterns.
- **"tell ~" for "talk about / give / say"** appears again in goal and wrap-up lines (L431, L438, L439, L440). Expect it in L441-450 too.
- **"Congratulations, everyone!" as an opener** (L435, L440) seems to be a template. Check other lessons' teacherScript openings.
- **Bare "ing" fill-in models** ("are... ing", "the... is... ing") are fixed in L438 and L439. L440 seg17 "In the picture... I think..." has no bare suffix and is kept.
- **JA かれ / かのじょ** (L436 expressions, lines and subtitles) is kept, because many worlds use it and earlier reviews kept it. If the owner wants kid-natural JA, おとこのこの / おんなのこは is the change.
- Kept on purpose: the quiz distractors "He'm reading", "They isn't running", "Kiko and Birimo is jumping rope", "The red team does"; Birimo's self-answering "Who's winning now?" (L434); "Great reporting today!" (L438); "Haru, please start." (L440); "n-apostrophe-t" (L439 script); チャンピオン for winner (L434 seg19 JA).

### Run 9 review notes: L441-450 (world 15, Stage 2)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide). I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (double end marks, quiz "A / B" subtitles, "Yes, I am! Or no, I'm not." says, OK, thumbs-up, US praise, L445 "That's so nice", Ding-dong, "Last time", "speed challenge", lowercase after "Now, / Then, / Next, / First,"). There are 35 patches in 9 lessons. L447 has no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no title changes. The English in this block is already clean and child-friendly, so most fixes are small. Themes: (1) "Let's say it." with no model sentence after it in the teacherScript (L441, L443); (2) odd wording a teacher would not say ("It's evening in the tent", "Turn it over" for a page, "kite ride", "tell every day from right now", "Tell about your picture"); (3) TTS and reading problems (a bare heteronym "Live.", "And now. Freeze!", a stacked-comma recap line); (4) a fact slip ("Sunflowers always face the sun"); (5) unnatural Japanese (くうきに かく, かく ひとたち, かきてたち); (6) one capital after "Finally," that the block fix did not cover. Every new English sentence has 12 words or fewer. The check script passes: ALL OK. The touched lessons are 216-227 s long with 97-98% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L441 A Rainy Afternoon
- A: The rainy tent scene is warm and natural ("Drip, drop", "Here, Birimo. Sit next to me. It's dry here."). In the script, "Let's say it." is followed by nothing, and the next line is "OK, everyone, ...". The quiz "It's raining, so which is right?" makes the weather sound like the reason for the grammar. "Today is rainy, but tomorrow may be sunny." is fine ("might" is a little more natural, but this is a single flag).
- B: The y = ee rule for longer words is clear and matches the tipJa. Same flag on the quiz "so". Haru's "We're playing cards." vs JA トランプを しよう is close enough, because it reads as "that's what we're doing" (single flag, kept).
- C: The script stops dead after "Let's say it." The quiz line reads fine aloud. "i n g" spell-outs follow the central decision.
- Decision: teacherScript "Let's say it." + "Listen! The rain is getting stronger." (A+C). seg18 say/subtitleEn "It's raining, so which is right?" -> "It's raining. Which is right?" (A+B). The JA already has two sentences.

#### L442 Rainbow Over the Plains
- A: The after-rain scene is natural ("Shake off the rain. Like a puppy!", "Why is there a rainbow? Is it magic?"). "Coming out means appearing." uses a harder word, but it is OK as a gloss. "Rain water" is written as two words.
- B: The short-word y = eye rule and the "Last time, we learned rainy" link are good. The JA くうきに にじを かいて (tpr, seg11, seg13) is unnatural. Japanese says くうちゅうに かく.
- C: Reads well. "F, L, Y." is a clean letter spell-out. "Why do birds fly in the dry sky?" is a deliberate sound sentence and is easy to say.
- Decision: seg4 "Rain water" -> "Rainwater" (objective, spelling). tpr[1].ja and the seg11/seg13 subtitleJa くうきに -> くうちゅうに (objective, JA).

#### L443 Sunflower Field
- A: The field, the bee and Birimo's "I'm yellow, too!" are natural and fun. "Sunflowers always face the sun." is not true (only young plants follow the sun, and grown ones face east). The script also ends its tip with "Let's say it." and no sentence, like L441.
- B: The are/is contrast (many sunflowers / one bee) is clear. The script says "yeh" and the segments say "yuh" for initial y (block-wide note, kept).
- C: Same gap after "Let's say it." "Tongue up high, then slide. Yuh!" is readable.
- Decision: teacherScript "Sunflowers always face the sun." -> "Sunflowers love the sun." (objective, fact), and add "Wow, it's taller than me!" after "Let's say it." (A+C).

#### L444 Night on the Plains
- A: The quiet campfire night is lovely ("Shh, let's use quiet voices tonight.", "I wish for a big cake. Is that OK?"). seg2 defines the singular word "Star" as "Tiny lights in the dark sky".
- B: Same flag on seg2 (the word is singular, so the definition should be too). The three-y-sounds review is clear. The "lean back in your chair" option keeps the TPR doable.
- C: Reads softly and naturally. "A shy young owl in the starry sky." is easy to say.
- Decision: seg2 say/subtitleEn -> "Star. A tiny light in the dark sky." (A+B). The JA ちいさな ひかり is kept.

#### L445 Plains Diary
- A: The diary-sharing scene is natural, and Haru's cookie joke works. seg0 "It's evening in the tent." is odd. seg5 "Page. Turn it over." means flipping something upside down, but you turn a page. "Close your notebook. Flap!" is a wing sound.
- B: Same flags on seg0 and seg5. The JA こんばんは、かく ひとたち！ (seg0) and すごい かきてたち！ (seg13) are not natural Japanese for "writers". さっか is the natural word. The privacy note (no real name or address) is good.
- C: The recap "start a diary with Today, I'm, add the weather, and give a reason with because" stacks commas around a quoted fragment and cannot be read clearly. "Flap!" sounds wrong for a notebook. "Snap!" works.
- Decision: seg0 -> "It's evening, and we're in the tent." with JA さっかの みんな！ ゆうがた、テントの なかに いるよ。 (A+B). seg5 -> "Turn the page." (A+B). seg12 "Flap!" -> "Snap!" (A+C). seg13 subtitleJa かきてたち -> さっかたち (objective, JA). seg20 -> "You can start a diary with Today, I'm. Then add the weather and a reason with because." (A+C).

#### L446 Gesture Game
- A: The game is natural ("No words, only gestures!", "You got it, Riko! One point!"). Riko asking Haru and then Kiko ("Kiko, is he dancing?") is playful and fine.
- B: Are/Is question order, the short answers and the "Yes, I do" distractor are well chosen. The ing spelling review is correct.
- C: "Shake, shake! And now. Freeze!" puts a full stop where the suspense pause should be. The JA already has そして…. "Ring the bell at the end. Ing!" is fine.
- Decision: seg12 say/subtitleEn "And now. Freeze!" -> "And now... Freeze!" (A+C).

#### L447 Who Is It?
- A: The kite-festival guessing game is natural ("Hello, detectives!", "Case closed!", "The balloon is pulling me up!"). The wearing/holding contrast is how teachers explain it.
- B: The "be kind, don't say mean things about people" tip is good for describing people. The contractions and ing review are correct. EN and JA match.
- C: Reads well ("She's smiling. She isn't frowning!").
- Decision: no change.

#### L448 My Picture Sentences
- A: The studio scene and Birimo's "I'm eating three sandwiches, not one!" are fun. seg1 "Tell about your picture" is the known unidiomatic template.
- B: Same flag on "Tell about". The writing rules (capital letter, period, spaces) and the capital-I quiz fit the lesson. seg12 "Finally, Show your work." has a capital mid-sentence that the block fix (Now/Then/Next/First) did not cover.
- C: "A, in my picture, with a small i." may make TTS say "eye", which is the letter name, so it is OK. The rest reads well.
- Decision: seg1 "Tell about" -> "Talk about" (A+B). seg12 "Finally, Show" -> "Finally, show" (objective).

#### L449 Windy Plains Review
- A: The goodbye picnic and the wind feather are a warm ending. seg12 "One more kite ride!" is wrong, because nobody rides a kite (JA もう いちど たこあげ). The recap "tell every day from right now" uses the "tell A from B" idiom, which kids will not get and which sounds like "say every day". TPR "Blow it up high." can also be heard as "blow it up" (explode). It is a learning item and is clear in context, so it is not an objective error (single flag, kept, reported).
- B: Same flag on the recap idiom. The habit-vs-now quiz is correct. The seg13 slide title "Let's move!" breaks the "Speed challenge" title used in every other lesson.
- C: "One more kite ride!" is fine to say but means the wrong thing. "Wind masters!" is fine.
- Decision: seg12 -> "One more time with the kite!" (A+B). seg13 slide.title -> "Speed challenge" (objective, consistency). seg20 -> "You can ask what people are doing. And you can tell every day and right now apart." (A+B).

#### L450 World 15 Grand Review
- A: The live show, Birimo's runaway kite and the ruins cliffhanger work well. "Next is World 16, Ruins of Memory!" matches the L451 opening.
- B: The three-step report (Look! / who is doing what / Keep going!) is clear, and is/are for one or two people is a good final check. The vocab "live" (adjective, /laɪv/) can be confused with the verb live.
- C: In seg4, the bare "Live." at the start and the end is a heteronym. TTS will likely read it as /lɪv/. Putting it in the phrase "live show" makes the right sound more likely.
- Decision: seg4 say/subtitleEn -> "Live. Like a live show! It's happening right now, at this moment. ... Live show!", JA ライブショーの live だよ！ ... live show！ (B+C). The vocab item "live" is unchanged.

#### Block-wide notes
- **Initial y is written "yeh" in some places and "yuh" in others**: teacherScripts use "yeh" (L443, L444, L445, L449) and segments use "yuh" (L443 seg9/seg20, L444 seg9, L445 seg9), while L449 seg9 uses "yeh". TTS reads these differently (/jɛ/ vs /jʌ/). Pick one form for the whole block (I'd suggest "yuh", because it is closest to /j/). Not changed here.
- **"Let's say it." with nothing after it** in teacherScripts: fixed in L441 and L443. Check the same tip template in other worlds.
- **"Tell about ~"**: fixed in L448 seg1. It still appears in other blocks' goal lines.
- **TTS heteronym "live"** (L450 vocab): the lone word "Live." can still be misread. If the TTS supports a pronunciation hint or lexicon, add /laɪv/ for this vocab card.
- **"Blow it up high."** (L449 tpr[1]): kept, because it is a learning item and not an objective error. Changing it to "Blow it high into the air." would need an ID change across tpr, seg11, seg13 and slides.
- **"Finally, Show"** (L448): capital after "Finally," was not covered by the Now/Then/Next/First mechanical fix. Scan other blocks for "Finally, [A-Z]" and "At the end, [A-Z]" mid-sentence.
- Kept on purpose: L441 "tomorrow may be sunny"; L441 Haru's "We're playing cards." (JA しよう); L442 "Coming out means appearing."; L446 "Kiko, is he dancing?"; L448 "with a small i"; L445 "Tap, tap, tap" for writing.

### Run 9 review notes: L451-460 (world 16, Stage 2)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide). I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (double end marks, quiz "A / B" subtitles, "Yes, I did. Or no, I didn't." says, OK, thumbs-up, US praise, Ding-dong, "Last time" in L451, "speed challenge", lowercase after "Now, / Then,") or the block conventions (the quiz "blank" placeholder, letter names like "E D", Japanese in `say` ending with "."). There are 51 patches in 9 lessons. L453 has no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no title changes. The most common fix is objective Japanese: "sing の むかしは sang" / "とくべつな むかしの ことば" uses むかし ("long ago") to mean "past form", in L456-L460 seg1 and L456 seg20. I changed it to "sing は sang" and すぎた かたち, the block term. Other fixes: "tell your weekend" / "Tell me your diary" (unidiomatic, L455 and L460), clipped or non-English teacher lines ("so is", "Answer for you", "Just add.", "Now, I know!", "Shut!"), "a plains pancake" (L452), a tense and "either" error in the L458 riddle chat, and Ms. Emma calling herself "Ms. Emma" in the L459 script. Every new English sentence has 12 words or fewer. The check script passes: ALL OK. The touched lessons are 217-226 s long with 97-99% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L451 The Old Ruins
- A: The arrival at the ruins is natural ("Look! Stone walls and a tall tower!", "Maybe a thousand years ago."). In seg8, "That's the past, so lived. ... That's true now, so is." is clipped. A teacher would say "so we say lived / so we say is".
- B: The e + d rule and the /d/ vs /t/ ending are correct (lived, smiled /d/; liked, danced /t/). The past vs present contrast in seg8 is a good point. In seg17 subtitleJa, "き の うえの いえ" has a broken space.
- C: "That's true now, so is." sounds like "so is (she)" = "me too" when read aloud. The rest reads well ("Swish, swish!", "Look up, way up to the top!").
- Decision: seg8 say/subtitleEn -> "so we say lived" / "so we say is" (A+C). seg17 subtitleJa き の うえ -> きの うえ (objective, JA).

#### L452 What Did You Do Yesterday?
- A: The morning chat about yesterday is natural ("They were heavy!", "Aw, Kiko."). "I tried a plains pancake." is not an English compound. "the live show" refers to the World 15 live show (L450), so it is kept. "Flip it in the pan. Hop!" is a slightly odd sound word (single flag, kept).
- B: The y -> i + ed rule is correct, and "Was it fun?" as a follow-up is a good next step. Same flag on "plains pancake": the noun "plains" used as an adjective is unclear for A1. "Bag ... carry it on your back" describes a backpack, but it fits the trip (single flag, kept). "Did shows the past, so do stays plain" is OK.
- C: "Riko blank the map yesterday" uses the block quiz convention (kept). "live show" right after the live/lived lesson could trip TTS, but the context ("the live show") makes it /laɪv/ (kept).
- Decision: conversation.lines[2] en/ja, seg6 say/subtitleEn/subtitleJa/slide item -> "I tried a pancake on the plains." (A+B).

#### L453 We Stopped and Looked
- A: The dark painting room is natural ("Shh, we're inside the ruins now.", "Then, clunk!", "Phew!"). "Long ago, people painted their happy days on walls." is fine.
- B: The doubling rule (short vowel + one consonant) and the /t/ ending of stopped/clapped/hopped/dropped are correct. The link to -ing doubling in the script is a good recycle. EN and JA match.
- C: Reads well. "Two Ps every time." may come out as "two P S" in some voices (single flag, kept).
- Decision: no change.

#### L454 Did You See That?
- A: The lizard scene is natural ("Did you see that? Something moved!", "Bye, little lizard!"). In seg17, "Answer for you." is not English.
- B: In seg1, "did の あとは ed なし！" is followed by "Did you see, not Did you saw". Saw has no ed, so the rule contradicts its own example. seg19 ("the action word stays plain") states it correctly. seg8 "After did, no E D. Did you see? Did you hear?" is true for regular verbs (single flag, kept). Same flag on "Answer for you" (JA じぶんの こたえ).
- C: "Answer for you." sounds broken aloud. The four-way ed review ("Just add it. Just D. Double the G. Y to I.") reads well.
- Decision: seg1 say/subtitleJa "ed なし" -> "もとの かたち" (A+B). seg17 say/subtitleEn "Answer for you." -> "Give your own answer." (A+B+C).

#### L455 Ruins Diary, Day One
- A: The tent diary scene is warm ("Who wants to go first?", "Just a little!"). "Tell me your diary!" in aiTask is unidiomatic. "Close the door. Shut!" uses a verb where a sound word is expected. "We clapped at the big painting" is a cute kid line (kept).
- B: The was/were point and the four ed rules are clear. Same flag on "Tell me your diary". The privacy tip fits.
- C: "Walk, walked. Just add." sounds cut off (compare L454 "Just add it."). "Shut!" barks like an order right after "Close the door."
- Decision: seg9 say/subtitleEn "Just add." -> "Just add it." (A+C). seg12 say/subtitleEn "Shut!" -> "Bang!" (A+C, JA バタン). aiTask.starterEn "Tell me your diary!" -> "Read me your diary!" (A+B).

#### L456 We Sang by the Fire
- A: The campfire memory is natural ("That was me! Sorry. The sound was so beautiful."). In seg7, "Then, ding, dong!" differs from the lesson's "Ding-dong!" everywhere else.
- B: The i -> a rule (sang, swam, drank, rang) and "Did you sing? Not Did you sang." are correct. In seg1, "sing の むかしは sang" uses むかし ("long ago") for "past form", and seg20 "とくべつな むかしの ことば" reads as "special old words". Both are wrong meaning in JA.
- C: "ding, dong" with a comma makes two separate hits. The hyphen gives one bell sound. The rest reads well ("Gulp, gulp!").
- Decision: seg1 say/subtitleJa -> "sing は sang! drink は drank!", "とくべつな すぎた かたち" (objective, JA). seg20 subtitleJa -> "とくべつな すぎた かたち" (objective). seg7 say/subtitleEn "ding, dong" -> "ding-dong" (A+C).

#### L457 I Wrote a Letter
- A: The old mailbox scene is natural ("A mail carrier comes every Monday.", "Here we go!"). In the script, "We'll practice words like this soon." is confusing in the very lesson that practices them. "Wiggle your pencil!" is a little odd for writing (single flag, kept).
- B: Same flag on "We'll practice ... soon". The /æ/ vs magic-e /eɪ/ point in gave is correct and clear. seg1 has the same むかし problem as L456.
- C: Reads well ("Slide!", "Bye-bye, letter!").
- Decision: teacherScript "We'll practice words like this soon." -> "It's a special one, with no ed." (A+B). seg1 say/subtitleJa "write の むかしは" -> "write は" (objective).

#### L458 Birimo Knew the Answer
- A: The riddle door is fun. But in conversation.lines[2], Riko says "I didn't know the answer, either." while the riddle is still unsolved. The right form is "I don't know", and "either" has no negative before it (Haru only guessed "A box?"). seg3 "The question asks, and the answer tells." is an odd definition. seg11 "Now, I know!" should be "Oh, I know!".
- B: Same flag on lines[2]: it models "didn't know" for a present state, the exact usage the lesson must keep clear. The target "I didn't know the answer." stays in expressions, seg15, seg19, the slides and the recap. The seg3 definition is abstract for A1. seg1 has the same むかし problem.
- C: "Now, I know!" has the wrong ring for the eureka moment. The riddle reads well. "Rumble, rumble." is good.
- Decision: conversation.lines[2] en/ja, seg6 say/subtitleEn/subtitleJa/slide item -> "Hmm. I don't know the answer." / うーん。こたえが わからないなあ。 (A+B). seg3 say/subtitleEn/subtitleJa -> "Someone asks a question, and you give the answer." (A+B). seg11 say/subtitleEn "Now, I know!" -> "Oh, I know!" (A+C). seg1 say/subtitleJa "know の むかしは" -> "know は" (objective). phonics.tipJa "swimed" vs seg9 "swimmed" (two different wrong forms) is kept.

#### L459 Where Did You Go Last Weekend?
- A: The Sana role-play is natural ("Sounds fun! Was it hot?", "I saw a big shark!"). In the script, "Ms. Emma will play the guide's daughter" has Ms. Emma talking about herself in the third person. seg0 already says "I'm going to play her!".
- B: "I stayed home." as the no-trip answer is a kind, inclusive choice. "Go becomes went, a totally new word" is clear. seg1 has the same むかし problem.
- C: The third-person self-reference sounds strange in Ms. Emma's voice. The tour TPR reads well ("Lean in and squint.").
- Decision: teacherScript "Ms. Emma will play" -> "I'll play" (A+C). seg1 say/subtitleJa "go の むかしは" -> "go は" (objective).

#### L460 Weekend Talk
- A: The rest-area chat is natural ("But I let it go.", "The birds sang, too!"). "Tell your weekend" (seg1, seg20) is unidiomatic. "You caught that one like a fish!" (seg19) is a confusing image. The natural fishing pun is "Nice catch!".
- B: Same flag on "tell your weekend". The three-step frame (where, what, how) is clear. In seg6 subtitleJa, "とまって おひるを たべた" is a literal "stopped". conversation.lines[3].ja already says ひとやすみして. seg1 has the same むかし problem.
- C: seg19 "like a fish" makes listeners picture the child as the fish. "Nice catch!" lands cleanly.
- Decision: seg1 say/subtitleEn and seg20 say/subtitleEn "tell your weekend" -> "talk about your weekend" (A+B). seg1 say/subtitleJa "catch の むかしは" -> "catch は" (objective). seg6 subtitleJa とまって -> ひとやすみして (objective). seg19 say/subtitleEn/subtitleJa -> "Nice catch!" / ナイス キャッチ！ (A+B).

#### Block-wide notes
- **"～ の むかしは ～" / "むかしの ことば"** (むかし = long ago, used for "past form"): I fixed 11 fields in L456-L460. A grep of world-16.json finds 24 matching lines, so about 13 remain in L461 and later. Fix them the same way ("sing は sang", "すぎた かたち"). Also check world-40.json (1 hit). Keep "むかし の ことは ed!" in L451, because there it really means "long ago".
- **"tell + noun" for "talk about"** (L460 "tell your weekend", L455 "Tell me your diary") is the same template as in W13-14. Expect it in other goal and wrap-up lines.
- **Riddle and conversation tense**: "I didn't know" for something still unknown (L458) is a trap in lessons that teach "didn't". Check later know/knew lessons.
- **Kept on purpose**: the quiz "blank" placeholder (block convention); "the live show" (L452, refers to the W15 live show); "Hop!" for a pancake flip; "Two Ps every time" (L453); "We clapped at the big painting" (L455); "Wiggle your pencil!" (L457); seg8 "After did, no E D" (L454); the phonics.tipJa "swimed" vs "swimmed" spelling of the wrong form (L458).

### Run 9 review notes: L461-470 (world 16, Stage 2)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide). I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (double end marks, quiz "A / B" subtitles, OK, thumbs-up, US praise, Ding-dong, "Last time", "speed challenge", lowercase after "Now, / Then, / Next, / First,") or the central decisions (Well done!, Japanese in `say` ending with ".", letter spell-outs like "E W" and "O U G H T", TPR capitals after "And,"). The L467 "First, ... Then, ... After that, ..." story pattern is kept as intended. There are 25 patches in 9 lessons (L468 has no change). There are no learning-item ID changes and no title changes. The most common fix is again "tell ~" used for "talk about" (L462 "tell a funny mistake", L467 "Tell your morning"). Others: a wrong grammar rule in L462 ("for was, ask with were"), a false cross-reference in the L467 script (World 14 cooking taught "First / Next", not "then / after that"), double-word stumbles read aloud ("Then and, and the last one" in L465, "Then use first, then" in L470), a pronoun with no referent in L466 seg6, a spoken quiz blank "hmm" in L464, and small EN/JA or frame mismatches (L465 goalJa, L469 Haru's line). Every new English sentence has 12 words or fewer. The check script passes: ALL OK. The touched lessons are 208-228 s long with 97-99% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L461 I Bought a Map
- A: The souvenir shop is natural ("Take your time, Haru.", "It was only two hundred yen."). "Here we are, the souvenir shop." reads as a fragment. A teacher says "Here we are at the souvenir shop." The quiz "Yesterday at the shop..." is story time, not a reference to the previous lesson, so it is right to keep it.
- B: buy -> bought and "did + buy" (seg8) are clear, and the o_e magic-e link (write -> wrote) is well explained. "buy の むかしは bought" uses the block's むかし wording (kept). EN and JA match.
- C: Reads well. The "Here we are, the souvenir shop" comma makes a flat, odd pause. "The I changes to O" is fine for TTS, because the letter name and the pronoun sound the same.
- Decision: conversation.lines[0].en -> "Here we are at the souvenir shop. You can buy one thing each." (A+C).

#### L462 I Thought It Was a Ghost!
- A: The sheet "ghost" is fun and natural ("Eek! Is it a ghost?", "Phew!"). In seg8, "And for was, ask with were." is a wrong rule. The script itself asks "Was it a ghost?" Were is used because of you. In seg20, "You can tell a funny mistake" is unidiomatic.
- B: Same flag on seg8, because it teaches a false rule right when was/were questions are new (the JA was の しつもんは were has the same error). Same flag on "tell a funny mistake". "A spooky friend in stories" vs JA ふしぎな ともだち is a mild mismatch (single flag, kept).
- C: Reads well ("Shh, shh.", "One, two, three, whoosh!"). "Oh, oh, oh!" is fine.
- Decision: seg8 say/subtitleEn -> "And with you, use were.", JA -> you の ときは were を つかうよ (A+B). seg20 "tell a funny mistake" -> "talk about a funny mistake" (A+B).

#### L463 Who Taught You?
- A: The pot-maker role play is natural ("I did. I made it last week.", "Thank you for teaching us!"). In seg1, "Or short, My grandpa did." is clipped. The script already says "Or say it short".
- B: Who + past and the short answer "~ did." are introduced clearly. The silent k in knew is a good extra. "Make a cup" in a pot workshop is a small shift (single flag, kept).
- C: "Or short," stumbles because it sounds like a missing word. "Push your lips out. Oo." is OK for TTS. "E W" spell-out follows the block decision.
- Decision: seg1 say/subtitleEn "Or short," -> "Or say it short," (A+C).

#### L464 It Wasn't Easy
- A: The tower climb is natural ("Ninety-eight, ninety-nine... one hundred!", "You didn't give up."). "one hundred stairs" is normal US speech. In seg18, the quiz gap "The stairs, hmm, wide." is not how a teacher marks a blank. Teachers say "blank".
- B: wasn't/weren't and "stairs means many steps" are clear. "Stairs is more than one" talks about the word, so it is fine. "The very highest place" is OK (single note).
- C: "hmm" sounds like the teacher is thinking, not like a gap, so the cloze is lost aloud. "E W again, oo." is a little choppy but readable.
- Decision: seg18 say/subtitleEn "hmm" -> "blank" (A+C). The JA ＿＿ already matches.

#### L465 A Day at the Ruins
- A: The evening look-back is warm ("Maybe it opens something!", "You found the key to this quiz!"). In seg8, "Then and, and the last one." sounds garbled. "treasure box" is less usual than "treasure chest" in US English (learning item, kept, reported).
- B: The A, B, and C rule is clear, and the quiz contrast works. The aiTask goalJa frame "We ～ed, we ～, and we ～." does not match targetExpressions[0] and the slide ("We ～ed, we ～ed, and we ～ed.").
- C: The double "and" in seg8 cannot be phrased clearly. The rest reads well ("Team oh ... Team oo").
- Decision: seg8 say/subtitleEn -> "Then and before the last one." (A+C). The JA それから and と さいごの ひとつ is kept. aiTask.goalJa frame -> "We ～ed, we ～ed, and we ～ed." (objective).

#### L466 Once Upon a Time
- A: The stone-tablet story is natural ("Lonely? Didn't he have any friends?", "No, he didn't."). In seg6, "Riko asks, Can you read them, Ms. Emma?" cuts her first sentence, so "them" has nothing to point to. "Royal!" as praise is a little odd (single flag, kept).
- B: Same flag on "them", because a child hears "read them" with no words mentioned. "No, he didn't" + ええ、いなかったの is correct for a negative question. "Once upon a time" vs "One day" order is well taught.
- C: Reads well ("Bling, bling!", "Open your mouth tall. Aw."). The "O U G H T" spell-out follows the block decision.
- Decision: seg6 say/subtitleEn -> "Riko says, Look, there are words on this stone. Can you read them, Ms. Emma? ...", and JA adds みて、この いしに もじが ある。 (A+B).

#### L467 First, Then, After That
- A: The seed story and "It's like a recipe!" are natural. The script tip "Remember the cooking class in World 14? First, then, after that." is false. World 14 (L401) taught "First, ~. Next, ~." In seg17, "Tell your morning in order" is unidiomatic. In seg12, "After that, Plant" and "Finally, Water" keep TPR capitals mid-sentence, while seg11 already uses lowercase "First, open / Then, look".
- B: Same flag on "Tell your morning". The aught/ought "same sound" point is clear. Keeping the "First, ... Then, ..." commas is intended.
- C: "Aw-t." may be read as "aw tee" by TTS (single flag, kept, reported). Otherwise reads well ("Green thumbs!").
- Decision: teacherScript tip -> "Remember the cooking class in World 14? We said First and Next. Stories use order words, too!" (objective, fact). seg17 "Tell your morning" -> "Talk about your morning" (A+B). seg12 -> "After that, plant a seed. ... Finally, water the plant." (objective, matches the block's lowercase rule).

#### L468 Finally, He Smiled
- A: The happy ending is natural ("The end! That's a happy story.", "I think the little bird knew the king was lonely."). "The seed grew and grew. A big tree grew next to the castle." uses grew three times, but it is storybook style and the ew review word (single flag, kept). "Smile big." is fine in US English.
- B: "Finally" plus "not ... anymore" is one clear new step. The quiz "He was lonely anymore" is an intended wrong option. EN and JA match.
- C: Reads well ("Crouch down small. Grow like a tree. Up, up, up!").
- Decision: no change.

#### L469 How Did He Feel?
- A: The feelings talk is natural ("He didn't expect a box!", "I felt warm."). "Show me surprised." is classroom-speak (a learning item, so it is kept and reported). "Wow, speedy tongue!" is a little odd (single flag, kept).
- B: did + feel / felt + because is clear. Haru's line "He felt sad." has JA かなしかったと おもう, which adds an "I think" that is not in the English (only Kiko says "I think"). This mismatch is also in seg6 subtitleJa.
- C: The fast ought/aught round is intentional and readable. "aw-t" is the same TTS note as L467.
- Decision: conversation.lines[1].ja and seg6 subtitleJa かなしかったと おもう -> かなしかったよ (objective, EN/JA).

#### L470 Story Time at the Ruins
- A: The story circle is natural ("Me! Once upon a time, a little dragon lived in the tower.", "Great story, Kiko! My turn next!"). In seg1, "Then use first, then, and after that." puts "then" twice in a row. "Speak loudly." is less natural than "Speak up." (learning item, kept).
- B: The train metaphor (engine, cars, last car) is a good summary. The four-word review across three sounds is clear.
- C: In seg1, "Then use first, then" blurs the list aloud. The rest reads well.
- Decision: seg1 say/subtitleEn "Then use first, then, ..." -> "Next, use first, then, and after that." (A+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **"tell ~" for "talk about"** shows up again (L462 seg20, L467 seg17). "Tell a story" stays because it is idiomatic. Check other W15-16 goal/wrap-up lines for "tell a ~ / tell your ~".
- **Cross-world references in scripts** should be checked against the target world. L467's "World 14 cooking ... First, then, after that" was false (W14 taught First / Next). There may be similar "Remember ... in World N?" tips elsewhere.
- **TPR capitals after "After that, / Finally,"** mid-sentence (L467 seg12) were missed by the block lowercase fix, which covered only Now / Then / Next / First. I fixed L467. Consider adding "After that," and "Finally," to the block rule. The "And, Say ..." TPR capitals are kept as the block pattern.
- **"Aw-t"** (L467 seg9, L468 seg9, L469 seg9, L470 seg9) may be read as "aw tee" by TTS. It is kept (single flag), but it is worth a listen test.
- **Learning items kept but slightly non-native:** "treasure box" (L465, US usually says "treasure chest"), "Show me surprised." (L469 TPR), "Speak loudly." (L470 TPR). None are errors, and changing them would be ID changes. These are for the coordinator to decide.
- Kept on purpose: the "Yesterday" quiz framing in L461 (story time, not the previous lesson), "one hundred stairs" (L464), "Royal!" (L466), the "grew and grew ... grew" repetition in L468, and "speedy tongue" (L469).

### Run 9 review notes: L471-480 (world 16, Stage 2)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide). I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (double end marks, quiz "A / B" subtitles, "Yes, I did. Or no, I didn't." says, OK and "Are you OK now?" in L473, thumbs-up, US praise, Ding-dong, "Last time" in L472, "speed challenge", lowercase after "Now, / Then, / Next, / First,"). There are 46 patches in 9 lessons (L477 has no change). There are no learning-item ID changes and no title changes. The main themes: Ms. Emma narrating herself in the third person ("Ms. Emma says" in L475/L476, while L479 already says "I ask"); unidiomatic phrases ("keep a talk going", "tell your best day", "Trouble story", "Dolphins are happy news", "Thank the places you visit"); the Japanese "むかし" used as a name for the past form (L471, L474, L476), where this world otherwise says すぎた かたち; one grammar error ("O and a quiet E says"); one UK word ("bin"); and "Show time!" -> "Showtime!" (US spelling, same as L180/L405). Every new English sentence has 12 words or fewer. Shell tools were not available in this run, so I could not run check.ts. I checked by hand that every `before` string is in world-16.json, that the new slide item (L479 seg6) matches conversation.lines[3], and that L478, the lesson that grows most (+8 words), stays at about 225 s by the timing.ts formula. The coordinator should run check.ts before applying.

#### L471 Summer Stories
- A: The traveler role-play ("Let's pretend. I'm a traveler named Leo.") and the beach and fireworks lines are natural. In seg18, "Summer is over, so it's was." is not how a teacher says it. seg8 already has the natural form, "so we say was".
- B: "It was ～ + one more sentence + How about you?" is a clear three-step frame, and the three -ed sounds are introduced with good examples. In seg8 (say and subtitleJa), "was は is の むかし（の かたち）" names the past form むかし ("long ago"), which is unnatural and does not match すぎた かたち, the term this world uses everywhere (titles, slides, L476 to L480).
- C: seg18 "so it's was" stumbles (it's / was clash). "Want, wan-ted!" and the clapped extra beat read well. "E D" and "A soft d" are the block's letter spell-out pattern (kept).
- Decision: seg18 say/subtitleEn -> "Summer is over, so we say was." (A+C). seg8 say/subtitleJa "むかし（の かたち）" -> "すぎた かたち" (objective, JA).

#### L472 Who Did You Go With?
- A: The trip chat is natural ("What was the best part?", "We stayed up late and looked at the stars!"). seg19 "Now you can keep a talk going." is not the idiom. The usual phrase is "keep the conversation going". "In summer, I visited..." (instead of "This summer") is a little loose, but OK (single flag).
- B: The follow-up questions are a good next step after L471. In seg8, "did の あとは ed なし! Go, not went." uses a rule about ed to explain went, which has no ed. The real rule is the base form (もとの かたち). The same "ed なし" shorthand is fine in L475, where the example is learned -> learn.
- C: Reads well ("Smile, click!", "Bye-bye, sea!"). "Teeth on your lip for the F" could be "top teeth", but it is clear enough (single flag).
- Decision: seg8 say/subtitleJa "did の あとは ed なし" -> "did の あとは もとの かたち" (A+B). seg19 say/subtitleEn -> "Now you can keep the conversation going." (A+B).

#### L473 Sounds Fun! Oh No!
- A: The ice-cream story and Birimo's "Sounds fun! Well, not the first part." are funny and natural. The salt-in-tea opener is good. "Trouble story?" (seg1) and "trouble stories" (seg20) are not natural English. In seg19, "Dolphins are happy news." is illogical, because the swim is the news, not the dolphins.
- B: Same flag on "trouble story". seg8 and seg12 already use "Good news? / Bad news?", so seg1 and seg20 should use the same pair for one clear contrast. Same flag on seg19. "Are you OK now? ― Yes, I'm fine." as a later check-in is well placed.
- C: "Trouble story?" is clunky to say. The rest reads well ("Eyes wide! Oh!", "Oops, I dropped it!").
- Decision: seg1 "Happy story? ... Trouble story?" -> "Good news? ... Bad news?" in say/subtitleEn, with JA いい ニュース / わるい ニュース (A+B). seg20 "to happy stories ... to trouble stories" -> "to good news ... to bad news", with JA to match (A+B). seg19 -> "That's happy news.", JA それは うれしい ニュースだね (A+B). The teacherScript ("For a happy story / For a bad story") and testPoints JA are kept.

#### L474 My Best Day Ever
- A: The lookout scene and Birimo's "My best day is today!" are warm and natural. "Hello, my stars!" is Ms. Emma's recurring greeting (W9, W26, W40), so it is kept. seg20 "You can tell your best day" is unidiomatic, and it should be "talk about" (same fix as L382/L387/L389).
- B: Same flag on seg20. The event + feeling frame is clear. seg19 "was は is の むかし（の かたち）" has the same JA problem as L471.
- C: Reads well ("Shh, it's a secret.", "Whoosh!").
- Decision: seg20 say/subtitleEn "tell your best day" -> "talk about your best day" (A+B). seg19 say/subtitleJa -> "was は is の すぎた かたち" (objective, JA).

#### L475 Memory Interview
- A: The TV studio frame is natural ("Lights, camera, action!", "You're a pro interviewer!"). In seg6, Ms. Emma reads "Ms. Emma says, Riko, you're the interviewer." Teachers do not narrate themselves in the third person, and L479 seg6 already uses "I ask".
- B: The When / How was it pair is clear, and "after did, no E D. Learn, not learned." is correct here. phonics.tipJa uses the kanji 重ねる, which is above level, while the seg9 subtitle already writes かさねる.
- C: "Ms. Emma says" in Ms. Emma's own voice sounds odd. "Ask, asked. Just add it." is a little vague, but OK (single flag). "Uh-huh, uh-huh. Scribble, scribble!" reads well.
- Decision: seg6 say/subtitleEn "Ms. Emma says," -> "I say," with JA エマ先生「」 -> わたし「」 (A+C). phonics.tipJa 重ねる -> かさねる (objective, JA).

#### L476 Past Tense Quest
- A: The treasure-box password game is natural ("Click! The box is open! We did it!", "Ugh, it's heavy!"). seg6 has the same self-narration problem: "Ms. Emma says, Wait." "What's the past of buy?" is ordinary teacher talk (also in reviewed W12), so it is kept.
- B: The irregular forms come in small sets, and "Not goed. Never!" is a good contrast. seg8 "go の むかしは went" has the same むかし problem. In seg9, "O and a quiet E says oh." has a plural subject, so the verb should be say.
- C: Same flag on "Ms. Emma says". "goed", "eated" and "buyed" are intentional wrong forms (kept). The spell-outs "O U G H T" and "E W" are the block pattern.
- Decision: seg6 say/subtitleEn "Ms. Emma says," -> "I say," with JA わたし「」 (A+C). seg8 say/subtitleJa "go の むかしは" -> "go の すぎた かたちは" (objective, JA). seg9 say/subtitleEn "says oh" -> "say oh" (objective, grammar).

#### L477 Picture Story
- A: The four-picture story is natural ("Hey, that's me! But it's true.", "A tall man hurried over and caught it."). "Sun hat, warm hat" could be "winter hat", but the JA あったかい ぼうし matches (single flag).
- B: Yesterday / Then / Finally gives a clear beginning, middle, and end, and the -ed and aught review is correct. EN and JA match.
- C: Reads well ("Hold your hat! Tight!", "Whoosh! The hat is flying."). "Now you are the wind" could contract, but it is fine aloud.
- Decision: no change.

#### L478 My Diary Page
- A: The old diary scene is warm, and the ending "Maybe someday, someone will read ours, too." is lovely. In seg6, the diary lines are read with no speaker ("Let's listen. Today, I helped my mother." / "Kiko asks, Then what? Then I played..."), so they sound like Ms. Emma or Kiko talking about themselves. In seg5, "Big, old, and roar!" puts a sound inside an adjective list.
- B: The When / Where / What / Feeling diary frame is clear. A child cannot tell who "I" is in seg6, while the subtitleJa already labels the first quote にっき.
- C: seg6 needs a speaker cue before each diary line. seg5 "and roar" trips.
- Decision: seg6 say/subtitleEn adds "The diary says," before both diary lines, and the subtitleJa adds にっき before the second quote (A+C). seg5 -> "Big animals from long ago. Roar!", JA おおむかしの おおきな いきもの。ガオー！ (A+C).

#### L479 Ruins of Memory Review
- A: The goodbye tour is natural ("Duck down!", "Birimo blushes. Hee hee!"). Kiko's "The people danced in the picture." is odd, because a painting shows an ongoing scene ("The people in the picture were dancing."). The script tip "Thank the places you visit" is not natural English. "Into the bin!" is British, and US English says "trash can".
- B: Same flag on "danced in the picture". The JA おどってたね already means "were dancing". "Thank the places" is abstract for kids, and "Take care of the places" fits the trash TPR. The What / Where / Did you contrast and the "Yes, I liked!" quiz are correct.
- C: "Did you? gets yes or no." is terse, but readable (single flag). Spell-outs are the block pattern.
- Decision: conversation.lines[3], seg6 say/subtitleEn and seg6 slide.items[2] -> "I went to the wall painting. The people in the picture were dancing." (A+B). teacherScript "Thank the places you visit" -> "Take care of the places you visit" (A+B). seg4 "Into the bin!" -> "Into the trash can!" (objective, US usage).

#### L480 World 16 Grand Review
- A: The story show and the star-map cliffhanger work well ("Is that our next stop?"). In seg12, "Show time!" should be the US one-word form, "Showtime!" (block decision from L180/L405).
- B: The order words plus past forms are a good wrap-up. The tpr/actions match the earlier worlds.
- C: Reads well ("Chest out, big smile.", "A little bird sang, and it flew away!").
- Decision: seg12 say/subtitleEn "Show time!" -> "Showtime!" (objective, spelling).

#### Block-wide notes
- **"むかし" as the name for the past form**: also in the other half of world 16: "go の むかしは went!" (world-16.json, the L46x lesson with "Where did you go last weekend?", seg1 say/subtitleJa). Fix to すぎた かたち there too.
- **"did の あとは ed なし"**: this shorthand is fine when the example has ed (learned -> learn). It is misleading next to "Go, not went" (fixed in L472). Check the L46x "Did you see, not Did you saw." goal segment, where "ed なし" sits next to saw.
- **Kanji 重ねる in tipJa**: also in two L46x phonics tips (clap -> clapped, stop -> stopped). Use かさねる, as the subtitles do.
- **"Ms. Emma says / asks" in Ms. Emma's own narration**: fixed in L475/L476 here. The grep shows many more cases in W17, W20 and W26-40. Use "I say / I ask", as in L479.
- **"Show time!"** is still in world 24 (the segment "Show time! Hold up your travel poster."). Fix it to "Showtime!" when that block is reviewed.
- **L480 seg20** "with Once upon a time, First, then, and Finally" now has a lowercase "then" between capitalized quoted words. That comes from the block-wide "lowercase after First," fix applied to a list of quoted story words. I did not undo it. The coordinator may want to make it "Then" (the subtitleJa writes Then).
- Kept on purpose: "Hello, my stars!" (recurring greeting); "What's the past of buy / fly?" (normal teacher talk, also in reviewed W12); the intentional wrong forms (goed, eated, buyed, flied, "Yes, I liked!"); letter spell-outs ("E D", "O U G H T", "A U G H T"); L472 "In summer, I visited..."; L475 "Just add it."

| 422 | `teacherScript` | Hi, everyone! It's a perfect day for kites. The wind is strong, and the sky is blue. Riko is on the hill. Haru wants to know what she's doing. Today's sentence is: What are you doing? Say it with me. What are you doing? Good! And Riko answers, I'm flying a kite. Say it. I'm flying a kite. I'm is I am. I'm flying means I am flying right now. 「なにを しているの？」は What are you doing?。こたえは I'm ～ing. だよ。 Now, phonics. Ride, riding. Hide, hiding. Slide, sliding. Write, writing. Do you remember? When a word ends with e, take the e away and add i-n-g. Excellent! Here's a talking tip. When your friend tells you what they're doing, you can say, That looks fun! Let's say it. That looks fun! Now, let's grab our kites and run up the hill. Ready? Let's fly! | Hi, everyone! It's a perfect day for kites. The wind is strong, and the sky is blue. Riko is on the hill. Haru wants to know what she's doing. Today's sentence is: What are you doing? Say it with me. What are you doing? Good! And Riko answers, I'm flying a kite. Say it. I'm flying a kite. I'm is I am. I'm flying means I am flying right now. 「なにを しているの？」は What are you doing? こたえは I'm ～ing. だよ。 Now, phonics. Ride, riding. Hide, hiding. Slide, sliding. Write, writing. Do you remember? When a word ends with e, take the e away and add i-n-g. Excellent! Here's a talking tip. When your friend tells you what they're doing, you can say, That looks fun! Let's say it. That looks fun! | 422 | `teacher.segments[13].subtitleJa` | スピード チャレンジ！ わたしが きくから、やってみてね。なにを してるの？ いとを ひいて！ たかい ところを みて！ かぜと はしって！ たこを あげて！ すばらしい！ | スピード チャレンジ！ わたしが いうから、やってみてね。いとを ひいて！ たかい ところを みて！ かぜと はしって！ たこを あげて！ すばらしい！ | A+B | Follow seg13 say |
| 423 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today there's a race on the plains. Ready, set, go! Kiko is very fast. Haru is fast, too. When we talk about another person, we say he's or she's. Today's sentence is: Look! She's running fast. Say it with me. Look! She's running fast. Great! She's means she is. He's means he is. ほかの 人が いま している ことは、He's ～ing. / She's ～ing. で いうよ。 Start with Look! Then everyone looks at the same thing. This is how sports reporters talk. They're telling us what's happening now. Now, phonics. Dance, dancing. Race, racing. Chase, chasing. Bounce, bouncing. These words end with c-e or s-e. Take away the e and add i-n-g. Here's a cheering tip. When your friend is running, shout, Go, go, go! You can do it! Let's cheer together. Go, go, go! You can do it! Okay, reporters, get your voices ready. The race is starting! | Hello, everyone! Today there's a race on the plains. Ready, set, go! Kiko is very fast. Haru is fast, too. When we talk about another person, we say he's or she's. Today's sentence is: Look! She's running fast. Say it with me. Look! She's running fast. Great! She's means she is. He's means he is. ほかの 人が いま している ことは、He's ～ing. / She's ～ing. で いうよ。 Start with Look! Then everyone looks at the same thing. This is how sports reporters talk. They're telling us what's happening now. Now, phonics. Dance, dancing. Race, racing. Chase, chasing. Bounce, bouncing. These words end with c-e or s-e. Take away the e and add i-n-g. Here's a cheering tip. When your friend is running, shout, Go, go, go! You can do it! Let's cheer together. Go, go, go! You can do it! OK, reporters, get your voices ready. The race is starting! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 425 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today Ms. Emma is in the town, and you're on the plains. Let's call her on a video call. On a video call, your friend can't see everything. | 431 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! The children on the plains are playing soccer. They ask us to join them. Let's play! Today we're learning two kinds of sentences. Listen. | 433 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! There's a marathon on the plains today. Riko is running in it. It's a long race, and she's getting tired. Let's cheer for her. Grasshopper. Grass and hopper. A grasshopper is an insect that hops in the grass! Here's a tip. After the race, tell the runner, You did it! I'm proud of you! Let's say it. You did it! | 436 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today we're visiting a painter's studio on the plains. There are so many paintings on the walls! Ms. Emma is pretending to be the painter. Let's talk about the pictures. Today's sentence is: In the picture, a boy is reading. Say it with me. In the picture, a boy is reading. Great! Start with In the picture. Then say who is doing what. You can add There's. There's a dog next to him. え を せつめい するときは、In the picture, からはじめよう。 Now, new phonics. I am becomes I'm. He is becomes he's. She is becomes she's. It is becomes it's. The little mark is called an apostrophe. It shows that a letter is missing. Here's a tip for talking about pictures. You can say what you think, too. I think she's happy. Let's say it. I think she's happy. Okay, let's look at the first painting together! | Hello, everyone! Today we're visiting a painter's studio on the plains. There are so many paintings on the walls! | 437 | `teacherScript` | Hi, everyone! The painter has a very big picture today. It shows a festival on the plains. There are so many people in it! When a picture is big, tell us where to look. They are, they're. We are, we're. You are, you're. The a in are is missing. And let's review. She is, she's. Be careful! They're sounds like there, but the spelling is different. Here's a tip. If your friend is lost, help them. It's on the left, next to the tree. Let's look at the big picture now! | Hi, everyone! The painter has a very big picture today. It shows a festival on the plains. | 438 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today we're reading the Plains Newspaper. There's a big photo on the front page. Let's talk about it. Today's sentence is: What's happening in this picture? Let's say it. That's a good idea! OK, open the newspaper. What's happening today? | A+B / B+C | 'tell a reason' is unidiomatic, 'give a reason' (A+B); 'The i in is is missing' stumbles on 'is is', use seg9's 'goes away' (B+C) |

| 439 | `teacherScript` | Hi, everyone! Let's play Spot the Difference. Kiko has one picture, and Haru has another. They look the same, but there are five differences. Today's sentence is: In my picture, the cat is sleeping. Say it with me. In my picture, the cat is sleeping. Great! To tell a difference, say, In your picture, it isn't sleeping. It's eating. Isn't means is not. 「わたしの えでは」は In my picture、「あなたの えでは」は In your picture だよ。 For two or more, use aren't. The boys aren't running. Now, phonics. Is not, isn't. Are not, aren't. Do not, don't. Does not, doesn't. The o in not is missing. That's why we write n-apostrophe-t. Here's a game tip. When you find a difference, shout, Found one! Let's shout. Found one! Okay, look closely. | 440 | `teacherScript` | Congratulations, everyone! It's the Picture Talk Show at the studio. Each of you will talk about one picture. A good picture talk has three parts. First, In the picture, and what's happening. Next, people and what they're doing. Last, what you think. Today's sentences are: In the picture, it's raining. People are running. Say them with me. In the picture, it's raining. People are running. Excellent! Then add one more. A girl is holding an umbrella. I think she's kind. 3つの 文と、さいごに I think ～. で じぶんの かんがえ だよ。 Now, phonics review. I am, I'm. They are, they're. Is not, isn't. Let us, let's. Where did the letters go? The apostrophe tells you! Here's a tip for presenting. Point to the picture when you talk. Then everyone can follow you. Start with, Let's look at my picture. Okay, presenters, are you ready? Let's start the show! | Congratulations, everyone! It's the Picture Talk Show at the studio. | 444 | `teacherScript` | Good evening, everyone! It's night on the plains. The campfire is warm, and the sky is very dark. Shh, let's talk quietly. Look up. Today's sentence is: The stars are shining. Say it softly with me. The stars are shining. Beautiful! The moon is rising over the hill. Rising means going up. よるの ようすも ～ing で しずかに いえるよ。 The plains have no city lights, so we can see thousands of stars. Can you find a shooting star? Now, phonics. Sleepy. Starry. These say ee. Shy says eye. Young says yeh. Three sounds of y! Where is the y? At the start, it says yeh. At the end of a long word, ee. At the end of a short word, eye. Here's a tip for bedtime. When you're tired, say, I'm getting sleepy. Good night! Let's whisper it. I'm getting sleepy. Good night! Okay, let's lie down on the blanket and count the stars. | Good evening, everyone! It's night on the plains. The campfire is warm, and the sky is very dark. Shh, let's talk quietly. Look up. Today's sentence is: The stars are shining. Say it softly with me. The stars are shining. Now, phonics. Sleepy. Starry. These say ee. Shy says eye. Young says yeh. Three sounds of y! Where is the y? At the start, it says yeh. At the end of a long word, ee. At the end of a short word, eye. Here's a tip for bedtime. When you're tired, say, I'm getting sleepy. Good night! Let's whisper it. I'm getting sleepy. Good night! OK, let's lie down on the blanket and count the stars. | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 445 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! We've had many adventures on the plains. Tonight, let's write a Plains Diary in the tent, and read it to each other. A diary starts with Today. Today's sentence is: Today, I'm writing my diary in the tent. Say it with me. Today, I'm writing my diary in the tent. Great! Then write two more sentences. It's windy outside. My friends are sleeping. And add your feelings with because. I'm happy because I saw a rainbow. 日記は Today, から はじめて、いまの ようすと きもちを かこう。 Now, phonics review. Yes. Yeh. Happy. Windy. Ee. Fly. Eye. Can you hear all three sounds of y? Fantastic! Here's a tip. When your friend reads their diary, listen and say something nice. That's lovely! Let's say it. That's lovely! And remember, in a diary for sharing, you don't need to write your real name or address. Okay, take out your pencils! | Hello, everyone! We've had many adventures on the plains.
