| 446 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Let's play the Gesture Game. One person acts something out without words. Everyone else guesses what they're doing. To guess, ask a question. Today's sentences are: Are you swimming? No! I'm flying! Say them with me. Are you swimming? No! I'm flying! Great! If the guess is right, say, Yes, I am! If it's wrong, say, No, I'm not. I'm flying! はずれのときは No! I'm ～ing! で こたえよう。 You can ask about another person, too. Is he dancing? Yes, he is! Is she sleeping? No, she isn't! Now, phonics review. Swimming and hopping. Double the letter. Dancing. Drop the e. Flying. Just add i-n-g. Three rules again! Here's a game tip. When someone guesses right, say, You got it! Let's say it. You got it! Okay, who wants to act first? No talking, only gestures! | Hello, everyone! Let's play the Gesture Game. One person acts something out without words. Everyone else guesses what they're doing. To guess, ask a question. Today's sentences are: Are you swimming? No! I'm flying! Say them with me. Are you swimming? No! I'm flying! Great! If the guess is right, say, Yes, I am! If it's wrong, say, No, I'm not. I'm flying! はずれのときは No! I'm ～ing! で こたえよう。 You can ask about another person, too. Is he dancing? Yes, he is! Is she sleeping? No, she isn't! Now, phonics review. Swimming and hopping. Double the letter. Dancing. Drop the e. Flying. Just add i-n-g. Three rules again! Here's a game tip. When someone guesses right, say, You got it! Let's say it. You got it! OK, who wants to act first? No talking, only gestures! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 447 | `teacherScript` | Hi, everyone! There's a big kite festival on the plains today. There are so many people! Let's play Who Is It? Describe one person, and your friends find them. Today's sentences are: She's wearing a red hat. She's holding a kite. Say them with me. She's wearing a red hat. She's holding a kite. Wonderful! Wearing is for clothes, hats and glasses. Holding is for things in your hands. wearing は みに つけている もの、holding は てに もっている もの だよ。 You can also say what they're not wearing. He isn't wearing a hat. He's wearing glasses. Then ask, Who is it? Now, phonics review. She is, she's. Is not, isn't. Wear, wearing. Smile, smiling. Contractions and i-n-g words together! Here's a tip. Describe clothes and actions, but be kind. Don't say mean things about people. Okay, look at the crowd. Who is it? | Hi, everyone! There's a big kite festival on the plains today. There are so many people! Let's play Who Is It? Describe one person, and your friends find them. Today's sentences are: She's wearing a red hat. She's holding a kite. Say them with me. She's wearing a red hat. She's holding a kite. Wonderful! Wearing is for clothes, hats and glasses. Holding is for things in your hands. wearing は みに つけている もの、holding は てに もっている もの だよ。 You can also say what they're not wearing. He isn't wearing a hat. He's wearing glasses. Then ask, Who is it? Now, phonics review. She is, she's. Is not, isn't. Wear, wearing. Smile, smiling. Contractions and i-n-g words together! Here's a tip. Describe clothes and actions, but be kind. Don't say mean things about people. OK, look at the crowd. Who is it? | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 448 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today you're the artist. Draw a picture of the plains, and write three sentences about it. Today's sentence is: In my picture, Birimo is eating a sandwich. Say it with me. In my picture, Birimo is eating a sandwich. Great! Then add two more sentences. He's sitting under a sunflower. There's a rainbow in the sky. えを かいたら、In my picture, から 3つの 文を かこう。 Remember the rules for writing. Start with a capital letter. End with a period. Leave a space between words. Now, phonics review. Sunflower. Rainbow. Two words in one. It is, it's. Sit, sitting. We can use everything from this world! Here's a tip. Check your sentences with a friend. Ask, Can you read my sentences? Let's say it. Can you read my sentences? Okay, take out your crayons. Let's draw and write! | Hello, everyone! Today you're the artist. Draw a picture of the plains, and write three sentences about it. Today's sentence is: In my picture, Birimo is eating a sandwich. Say it with me. In my picture, Birimo is eating a sandwich. Great! Then add two more sentences. He's sitting under a sunflower. There's a rainbow in the sky. えを かいたら、In my picture, から 3つの 文を かこう。 Remember the rules for writing. Start with a capital letter. End with a period. Leave a space between words. Now, phonics review. Sunflower. Rainbow. Two words in one. It is, it's. Sit, sitting. We can use everything from this world! Here's a tip. Check your sentences with a friend. Ask, Can you read my sentences? Let's say it. Can you read my sentences? OK, take out your crayons. Let's draw and write! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 449 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! This is our last day on the Windy Plains. Let's have a goodbye picnic on the hill, and review everything. First, ask your friends, What are you doing? Then answer, I'm eating. I'm flying a kite. Say it with me. What are you doing? Great! Next, report something exciting. Look! A feather is falling from the sky. Remember, every day is for habits, and now is for right now. この ワールドでは、いま している ことを ～ing で いう れんしゅうを したね。 We learned place words, picture talk and cheering words, too. Behind. Between. In the picture. Keep going! Now, phonics review. Windy. Ee. Sky. Eye. Yellow. Yeh. Running. Double the n! Excellent! Here's a tip. At the end of a trip, tell your friends, This was so much fun! Let's say it. This was so much fun! Okay, let's spread the blanket. Wait, what's that in the sky? | Hello, everyone! This is our last day on the Windy Plains. Let's have a goodbye picnic on the hill, and review everything. First, ask your friends, What are you doing? Then answer, I'm eating. I'm flying a kite. Say it with me. What are you doing? Great! Next, report something exciting. Look! A feather is falling from the sky. Remember, every day is for habits, and now is for right now. この ワールドでは、いま している ことを ～ing で いう れんしゅうを したね。 We learned place words, picture talk and cheering words, too. Behind. Between. In the picture. This was so much fun! OK, let's spread the blanket. Wait, what's that in the sky? | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 450 | `teacherScript` | Congratulations, everyone! This is the last lesson of World 15. ワールド15 さいごの レッスンだよ。 First, speed TPR! Look! Stop! Come here! Go! Run! Hop! Swim! Fly! Great! Now, it's the World 15 live show on the plains stage. You are the reporters. Tell the audience what's happening right now. Today's sentence is: We're having a great time on the plains! Say it with me. We're having a great time on the plains! Wonderful! A good live report has three parts. Start with Look! Then say who is doing what. Look! Haru is flying a kite. Riko and Kiko are dancing. Then cheer. Keep going, everyone! Now, the last phonics review. Making. Running. Grasshopper. They're. Happy. Amazing! And look, the wind feather is glowing. It's showing us old stone ruins on the map. Where are we going next? But first, your live show. Speak loudly and smile. You can do it! | Congratulations, everyone! This is the last lesson of World 15. ワールド15 さいごの レッスンだよ。 First, speed challenge! Look! Stop! Come here! Go! Run! Hop! Swim! Fly! Great! Now, it's the World 15 live show on the plains stage. | 452 | `teacherScript` | Good morning, everyone! We're at the entrance of the ruins. Before we go in, let's talk about yesterday. To ask about yesterday, say, What did you do yesterday? Say it with me. What did you do yesterday? Great! Did is the past form of do. In the answer, we use a past form. I played soccer with my friends. I tried a new food. It was good! きのうの ことは did で きいて、played の ような すぎた かたちで こたえるよ。 Now, phonics. Cry, cried. Try, tried. Carry, carried. Study, studied. When a word ends in a consonant and y, change the y to i and add ed. Let's spell cried together. C, r, i, e, d. Good! Here's a tip. When your friend answers, ask one more question, like, Was it fun? Then it's a real conversation. Okay, let's ask our friends about yesterday! | Good morning, everyone! We're at the entrance of the ruins. Before we go in, let's talk about yesterday. | 453 | `teacherScript` | Hello, explorers! We're walking inside the ruins. It's a little dark, so we have flashlights. Suddenly, Riko says, Stop! There's a big painting on the wall. Later, we can say, We stopped and looked at the wall. Say it with me. We stopped and looked at the wall. Great! You can join two past actions with and. Stopped and looked. Walked and talked. Now, phonics. Stop, stopped. Clap, clapped. Hop, hopped. Drop, dropped. These words have a short vowel and end in one consonant. So we double the last letter and add ed. 2つ かさねる ルールは -ing の ときと おなじだね。 Here's a tip. In a dark place, walk slowly and stay with your group. Don't run! Okay, let's look at the painting together. What did the people do long ago? | Hello, explorers! We're walking inside the ruins. It's a little dark, so we have flashlights. Suddenly, Riko says, Stop! There's a big painting on the wall. | 454 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! We're in a long, dark passage in the ruins. Shh! What was that? Something moved! To ask a friend, say, Did you see that? Say it with me. Did you see that? Good! For a yes answer, say, Yes, I did. For a no answer, say, No, I didn't. Didn't means did not. Did ～? の しつもんには、Yes, I did. か No, I didn't. で こたえるよ。 Let's try one more. Did you hear a noise? No, I didn't. Now, phonics. Today we mix all the rules. Jump, jumped. Just add ed. Wave, waved. Just add d. Hug, hugged. Double the g. Hurry, hurried. Change y to i. Here's a tip. If you see a small wild animal, don't touch it. Just watch it quietly. Okay, let's find out what moved! | Hello, everyone! We're in a long, dark passage in the ruins. Shh! What was that? Something moved! To ask a friend, say, Did you see that? Say it with me. Did you see that? Good! For a yes answer, say, Yes, I did. For a no answer, say, No, I didn't. Didn't means did not. Did ～? の しつもんには、Yes, I did. か No, I didn't. で こたえるよ。 Let's try one more. Did you hear a noise? No, I didn't. Now, phonics. Today we mix all the rules. Jump, jumped. Just add ed. Wave, waved. Just add d. Hug, hugged. Double the g. Hurry, hurried. Change y to i. Here's a tip. If you see a small wild animal, don't touch it. Just watch it quietly. OK, let's find out what moved! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 455 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Our first day at the ruins is over. Tonight, let's write a ruins diary in the tent. In a diary, we write about things that already happened, so we use past forms. Today's sentences are: We walked to the ruins. We found a door. Say them with me. We walked to the ruins. We found a door. Great! Walked has ed, but found is a special past form. Find, found. 日記は すぎた ことだから、すぎた かたちで かくよ。 You can also use was and were. It was dark, but we weren't scared. Weren't means were not. Now, phonics review. Walked. Arrived. Clapped. Carried. Remember the four ed rules! Here's a tip. In a diary for sharing, you don't need to write your real name or address. Okay, let's read our diaries to each other! | Hello, everyone! Our first day at the ruins is over. Tonight, let's write a ruins diary in the tent. In a diary, we write about things that already happened, so we use past forms. Today's sentences are: We walked to the ruins. We found a door. Say them with me. We walked to the ruins. We found a door. Great! Walked has ed, but found is a special past form. Find, found. 日記は すぎた ことだから、すぎた かたちで かくよ。 You can also use was and were. It was dark, but we weren't scared. Weren't means were not. Now, phonics review. Walked. Arrived. Clapped. Carried. Remember the four ed rules! Here's a tip. In a diary for sharing, you don't need to write your real name or address. OK, let's read our diaries to each other! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 456 | `teacherScript` | Good morning, everyone! Last night, we all made a campfire together in the plaza. Do you remember? Today's sentence is: We sang a song by the campfire. Say it with me. We sang a song by the campfire. Wonderful! Sang is the past form of sing. Some verbs don't use ed. They change their spelling instead. sing の すぎた かたちは singed では なく、sang だよ。 Now, phonics. Sing, sang. Swim, swam. Drink, drank. Ring, rang. Can you hear it? The i changes to a. Let's say them again quickly. Sang, swam, drank, rang! Great! You can also ask a friend, Did you sing, too? Yes, I did. Here's a tip. A campfire is very hot, so always make one with a grown-up. Never play with fire. Okay, let's talk about our campfire night! | Good morning, everyone! Last night, we all made a campfire together in the plaza. Do you remember? Today's sentence is: We sang a song by the campfire. Say it with me. We sang a song by the campfire. Wonderful! Sang is the past form of sing. Some verbs don't use ed. They change their spelling instead. sing の すぎた かたちは singed では なく、sang だよ。 Now, phonics. Sing, sang. Swim, swam. Drink, drank. Ring, rang. Can you hear it? The i changes to a. Let's say them again quickly. Sang, swam, drank, rang! Great! You can also ask a friend, Did you sing, too? Yes, I did. Here's a tip. A campfire is very hot, so always make one with a grown-up. Never play with fire. OK, let's talk about our campfire night! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 457 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Look, there's an old mailbox near the ruins. And it still works! Last night, Riko wrote a letter. Today's sentence is: I wrote a letter to my grandma. Say it with me. I wrote a letter to my grandma. Great! Wrote is the past form of write. We'll practice words like this soon. A letter starts with Dear. Dear Grandma. And it often ends with Love, and your name. てがみの はじめは Dear ～, だよ。 Now, phonics. Begin, began. Give, gave. Sink, sank. Drink, drank. The i changes to a again. Here's a tip. On an envelope, we write an address. But on the internet or in a chat, never share your address. That's a safety rule. Okay, who will you write to? Let's write a short letter! | Hello, everyone! Look, there's an old mailbox near the ruins. And it still works! Last night, Riko wrote a letter. Today's sentence is: I wrote a letter to my grandma. Say it with me. I wrote a letter to my grandma. Begin, began. Give, gave. Sink, sank. Drink, drank. The i changes to a again. Here's a tip. On an envelope, we write an address. But on the internet or in a chat, never share your address. | 458 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today we found a big stone door. There's a riddle on it. I have many words, but I can't talk. You open me, but I'm not a door. What am I? Think hard! Birimo knew the answer. It's a book! Today's sentence is: Birimo knew the answer! Say it with me. Birimo knew the answer! Great! Knew is the past form of know. The k is silent, so we don't say it. know も knew も k は よまないよ。 If you didn't know, say, I didn't know the answer. Now, phonics. Swim, swam. Ring, rang. Sing, sang. Begin, began. The i changes to a. Here's a tip. When a friend gets the answer, say, Good job! And when you don't know, that's okay. Okay, let's make riddles for each other! | Hello, everyone! Today we found a big stone door. There's a riddle on it. I have many words, but I can't talk. You open me, but I'm not a door. What am I? Think hard! Birimo knew the answer. It's a book! Today's sentence is: Birimo knew the answer! Now, phonics. Swim, swam. Ring, rang. Sing, sang. Begin, began. The i changes to a. Here's a tip. When a friend gets the answer, say, Good job! And when you don't know, that's okay. OK, let's make riddles for each other! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 460 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! We're taking a break at the rest area of the ruins. Let's have a weekend talk. Today's sentences are: I went to the river. I caught a fish. Say them with me. I went to the river. I caught a fish. Great! Caught is the past form of catch. We'll learn more about it later. To keep talking, a friend can ask, What did you do there? Then answer with two or three past sentences. 2〜3文で はなすと、ほんとうの かいわに なるよ。 And add your feeling. It was exciting! Let's try. What did you do there? I played with my dog. It was fun! Now, phonics review. Swam. Sang. Stopped. Tried. Do you remember the rules? Here's a tip. Near a river, always stay with a grown-up, and don't go into deep water. Okay, let's share our weekends! | Hello, everyone! We're taking a break at the rest area of the ruins. Let's have a weekend talk. Today's sentences are: I went to the river. I caught a fish. | 461 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! There's a souvenir shop near the gate of the ruins. Souvenirs are things you buy on a trip to remember it. Today's sentence is: I bought a map at the shop. Say it with me. I bought a map at the shop. Great! Bought is the past form of buy. Buy, bought. It looks very different! To ask a friend, say, What did you buy? buy の すぎた かたちは bought。かたちが ぜんぜん ちがうね。 Now, phonics. Today we learn a new pattern. Write, wrote. Ride, rode. Drive, drove. Rise, rose. The i changes to o, and the word still ends with magic e. So the o says its name. Here's a tip. When you buy something, look at the clerk and say thank you. Okay, let's go shopping! | Hello, everyone! There's a souvenir shop near the gate of the ruins. Souvenirs are things you buy on a trip to remember it. Today's sentence is: I bought a map at the shop. | 462 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last night, something funny happened. Haru woke up and saw something white moving in the dark. Was it a ghost? No! It was just a sheet drying in the wind. Today's sentence is: I thought it was a ghost! Say it with me. I thought it was a ghost! Great! Thought is the past form of think. We use I thought when we were wrong about something. かんちがい した ときに つかえるよ。 You can also ask, Were you scared? Yes, I was. Let's practice. Were you scared? Yes, I was. Or, No, I wasn't. Now, phonics. Wake, woke. Break, broke. Speak, spoke. Choose, chose. Again, the past form has o and magic e. Here's a tip. If you feel scared at night, that's okay. Tell a grown-up. Okay, let's tell our funny stories! | Hello, everyone! Last night, something funny happened. Haru woke up and saw something white moving in the dark. Was it a ghost? No! | 463 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today we visit a pot maker's workshop at the ruins. Ms. Emma will play the pot maker. Look at these beautiful old patterns. To ask who taught someone, say, Who taught you? Say it with me. Who taught you? Great! Taught is the past form of teach. To answer, say, My grandpa taught me. Or say it short, My grandpa did. Who ～? の こたえは「～ did.」と みじかく いっても いいよ。 Let's practice. Who taught you? My mom taught me. My mom did. Now, phonics. Know, knew. Grow, grew. Throw, threw. Blow, blew. The past form ends in ew. It sounds like oo, as in moon. Here's a tip. When someone teaches you something, say, Thank you for teaching me! Let's say it. Thank you for teaching me! Okay, let's ask the pot maker! | Hello, everyone! Today we visit a pot maker's workshop at the ruins. Ms. Emma will play the pot maker. Look at these beautiful old patterns. To ask who taught someone, say, Who taught you? Say it with me. Who taught you? Great! Taught is the past form of teach. To answer, say, My grandpa taught me. Or say it short, My grandpa did. It sounds like oo, as in moon. Here's a tip. When someone teaches you something, say, Thank you for teaching me! Let's say it. Thank you for teaching me! OK, let's ask the pot maker! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 464 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Look up. Can you see the top of the tower? There are one hundred stairs! Let's climb. Today's sentence is: It wasn't easy, but I did it. Say it with me. | 465 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! What a busy day at the ruins! We climbed the tower, and at the top, we found an old key under a stone. Let's look back on our day. | 466 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today we found a big stone with old words on it. It's a story! Many stories start with Once upon a time. It means long, long ago. むかしむかし、と おなじ いみだよ。 Today's sentence is: Once upon a time, a king lived here. Say it with me. Once upon a time, a king lived here. Wonderful! Let's read on. He had a big castle, but he was lonely. One day, a little bird brought him a box. One day is another good story word. Now, phonics. Buy, bought. Think, thought. Bring, brought. Fight, fought. These past forms have ought. The g and h are silent. Here's a tip. When you listen to a story, make pictures in your head. Okay, let's read the story on the stone! | Hello, everyone! Today we found a big stone with old words on it. It's a story! Many stories start with Once upon a time. It means long, long ago. むかしむかし、と おなじ いみだよ。 Today's sentence is: Once upon a time, a king lived here. Say it with me. Once upon a time, a king lived here. Wonderful! Let's read on. He had a big castle, but he was lonely. One day, a little bird brought him a box. One day is another good story word. Now, phonics. Buy, bought. Think, thought. Bring, brought. Fight, fought. These past forms have ought. The g and h are silent. Here's a tip. When you listen to a story, make pictures in your head. OK, let's read the story on the stone! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 467 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Let's read more of the king's story. Stories happen in order, so we use special words to show the order. First. Then. After that. Today's sentences are: First, he opened the box. Then, he looked inside. Say them with me. First, he opened the box. Then, he looked inside. Great! What did he find? A tiny seed! After that, he planted the seed. じゅんばんを あらわす ことばを つかうと、おはなしが わかりやすく なるよ。 Let's say the order words again. First. Then. After that. To ask about a story, say, What happened next? Now, phonics. Catch, caught. Teach, taught. Buy, bought. Think, thought. Caught and taught have aught. Bought and thought have ought. They sound the same! Here's a tip. Remember the cooking class in World 14? First, then, after that. It's the same! Okay, let's read on! | Hello, everyone! Let's read more of the king's story. Stories happen in order, so we use special words to show the order. First. Then. After that. Today's sentences are: First, he opened the box. Then, he looked inside. Say them with me. First, he opened the box. Then, he looked inside. Great! What did he find? A tiny seed! After that, he planted the seed. じゅんばんを あらわす ことばを つかうと、おはなしが わかりやすく なるよ。 Let's say the order words again. First. Then. After that. To ask about a story, say, What happened next? Now, phonics. Catch, caught. Teach, taught. Buy, bought. Think, thought. Caught and taught have aught. Bought and thought have ought. They sound the same! Here's a tip. Remember the cooking class in World 14? First, then, after that. It's the same! OK, let's read on! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 468 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today we read the end of the king's story. The seed grew and grew into a big tree. Children came and played under it. The king brought them apples from the tree. Today's sentence is: Finally, the king smiled. Say it with me. Finally, the king smiled. Great! Finally means at the end. We use it for the last part of a story. Finally は「さいごに」。おはなしの おわりに つかうよ。 He wasn't lonely anymore. The end! Let's say the last part again together. Finally, the king smiled. The end! Now, phonics. Bring, brought. Fight, fought. Catch, caught. Teach, taught. Ought or aught? Look carefully at the spelling. Here's a tip. A good story ending makes people feel something. Happy, surprised, or warm inside. Okay, let's finish the story! | Hello, everyone! Today we read the end of the king's story. The seed grew and grew into a big tree. | 469 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Let's think about the king's feelings. To ask, say, How did he feel? Say it with me. How did he feel? Good! Felt is the past form of feel. He felt sad. He felt surprised. He felt happy. You can add a reason with because. He felt sad because he was lonely. きもちと りゆうを いえると、おはなしが もっと ふかく わかるよ。 At the start, he felt sad. At the end, he felt happy. Feelings can change in a story. Let's practice. How did she feel? She felt happy because she won the race. Now, phonics. Think, thought. Catch, caught. Buy, bought. Teach, taught. Let's say them fast. Thought, caught, bought, taught! Here's a tip. Thinking about feelings in stories helps us be kind to real people, too. Okay, let's talk about feelings! | Hello, everyone! Let's think about the king's feelings. To ask, say, How did he feel? Say it with me. How did he feel? Good! Felt is the past form of feel. He felt sad. He felt surprised. He felt happy. Thought, caught, bought, taught! Here's a tip. Thinking about feelings in stories helps us be kind to real people, too. OK, let's talk about feelings! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 470 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Tonight is story time in the plaza of the ruins. Each of us will tell a short story. Use these story words. Once upon a time. First. Then. After that. Finally. Today's sentence is: Once upon a time, a little dragon lived in the tower. Say it with me. Once upon a time, a little dragon lived in the tower. Great! First, it flew to the river. Then, it met a fish. Finally, they became best friends. Your story can be about anything. An animal, a robot, or a magic tree. おはなしの なかみは じゆう。すきな ものを だしてね。 Now, phonics review. Thought. Caught. Rode. Knew. Here's a tip. When a friend tells a story, listen quietly and clap at the end. Okay, who wants to go first? | Hello, everyone! Tonight is story time in the plaza of the ruins. Each of us will tell a short story. Use these story words. Once upon a time. First. Then. After that. Finally. Today's sentence is: Once upon a time, a little dragon lived in the tower. Say it with me. Once upon a time, a little dragon lived in the tower. Great! First, it flew to the river. Then, it met a fish. Finally, they became best friends. Your story can be about anything. An animal, a robot, or a magic tree. おはなしの なかみは じゆう。すきな ものを だしてね。 Now, phonics review. Thought. Caught. Rode. Knew. Here's a tip. When a friend tells a story, listen quietly and clap at the end. OK, who wants to go first? | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 471 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today we meet a traveler at the ruins. Birimo will play the traveler, Leo. Let's talk about summer vacation. To ask, say, How was your summer? Say it with me. How was your summer? Great! To answer, say, It was great! Or, It was fun! Then add one more sentence about what you did. I went to the mountains. And then ask back, How about you? How about you? と ききかえすと、かいわが つづくよ。 Now, phonics. Today, let's listen to the sounds of ed. Played. The ed sounds like d. Jumped. It sounds like t. Wanted. It sounds like id. Enjoyed. It sounds like d. Here's a tip. When you say jumped, the ed is short and quiet. Don't say jump-ed! Okay, let's share our summer stories! | Hello, everyone! Today we meet a traveler at the ruins. Birimo will play the traveler, Leo. Let's talk about summer vacation. To ask, say, How was your summer? Say it with me. How was your summer? Great! To answer, say, It was great! Or, It was fun! Then add one more sentence about what you did. I went to the mountains. And then ask back, How about you? How about you? と ききかえすと、かいわが つづくよ。 Now, phonics. Today, let's listen to the sounds of ed. Played. The ed sounds like d. Jumped. It sounds like t. Wanted. It sounds like id. Enjoyed. It sounds like d. Here's a tip. When you say jumped, the ed is short and quiet. Don't say jump-ed! OK, let's share our summer stories! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 472 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Yesterday we talked about summer. Today, let's ask more questions to learn more. These are called follow-up questions. One good question is, Who did you go with? Say it with me. Who did you go with? Great! The answer can be short. With my family. With my friends. Another question is, How long did you stay? For three days. もう ひとつ しつもんを すると、あいての はなしが もっと よく わかるよ。 Let's practice. Who did you go with? With my friends. How long did you stay? For two days. Now, phonics. Listen to the ed sounds. Visited, id. Stayed, d. Walked, t. Laughed, t. In laughed, g h sounds like f, and the ed sounds like t. Here's a tip. Ask questions because you are interested, and listen to the answers with a smile. Okay, let's ask follow-up questions! | Hello, everyone! Yesterday we talked about summer. Today, let's ask more questions to learn more. These are called follow-up questions. | 473 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Good listeners react to stories. Today, let's learn two reactions. For a happy story, say, Sounds fun! Say it with me. Sounds fun! | 475 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today we're reporters. Let's do a memory interview. To ask about the time, say, When did you learn to ride a bike? Say it with me. Today we're reporters. Let's do a memory interview. To ask about the time, say, When did you learn to ride a bike? Say it with me. When did you learn to ride a bike? Great! Answers can be short. When did you learn to swim? When I was six. How was it? It was fun! Now, phonics review. Asked. Worried. Nodded. Smiled. Do you remember the four ed rules? Here's a tip. In an interview, you don't need to say your real name. Just have fun and listen well. OK, let's start the interview! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

They help you talk about the past. すぎた ことを きく しつもんを ぜんぶ つかってみよう。 Let's practice. Where did you go first? I went to the tower. Did you like it? Yes, I did! Now, phonics review. Stopped. Began. Rode. Taught. We learned ed words and special past forms! Here's a tip. Thank the places you visit, and leave them clean for the next visitors. OK, let's start the goodbye tour! | Hello, everyone! This is our last tour of the Ruins of Memory. Let's look back at everything we did. Ask your friends, What did you do at the ruins? Where did you go first? Did you like it? Say it with me. What did you do at the ruins? Great! Remember these question starters. What did. Where did. Who did. And How was. They help you talk about the past. すぎた ことを きく しつもんを ぜんぶ つかってみよう。 Let's practice. Where did you go first? I went to the tower. Did you like it? Yes, I did! Now, phonics review. Stopped. Began. Rode. Taught. We learned ed words and special past forms! Here's a tip. Take care of the places you visit, and leave them clean for the next visitors. OK, let's start the goodbye tour! | A+B | 'Thank the places you visit' is odd in English and abstract for kids |

| 480 | `teacherScript` | Congratulations, everyone! This is the last lesson of World 16. ワールド16 さいごの レッスンだよ。 First, speed TPR! Look! Stop! Come here! Go! Sing! Swim! Write! Fly! Great! Now, it's the World 16 story show in the plaza. Today's sentence is: Once upon a time, four friends found a map. Say it with me. Once upon a time, four friends found a map. Wonderful! A good story uses order words. First. Then. After that. Finally. And it uses past forms. Walked. Found. Opened. Smiled. Now, the last phonics review. Lived. Sang. Flew. Thought. Wrote. Amazing! And look, the old diary has one more page. It's a map of the stars! Where are we going next? But first, your story show. Speak loudly and smile. You can do it! | Congratulations, everyone! This is the last lesson of World 16. ワールド16 さいごの レッスンだよ。 First, speed challenge! Look! Stop! Come here! Go! Sing! Swim! Write! Fly! Great! Now, it's the World 16 story show in the plaza. Today's sentence is: Once upon a time, four friends found a map. Say it with me. Once upon a time, four friends found a map. Wonderful! A good story uses order words. First. Then. After that. Finally. And it uses past forms. Walked. Found. Opened. Smiled. Now, the last phonics review. Lived. Sang. Flew. Thought. Wrote. Amazing! And look, the old diary has one more page. It's a map of the stars! Where are we going next? But first, your story show. Speak loudly and smile. You can do it! | B+C | kids do not know the term "TPR"; the say lines already use "speed challenge" (same as W13-14 L390/L420) |
